# São Paulo

This article is about the city. For the state, see [São Paulo (state)](229422.md). For other uses, see [São Paulo (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_\(disambiguation\)).

**São Paulo** ([/ˌsaʊ(m) ˈpaʊloʊ/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English);[^cite_note-8] Portuguese: [\[sɐ̃w ˈpawlu\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Portuguese) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/27/Br-SaoPaulo.ogg/Br-SaoPaulo.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/27/Br-SaoPaulo.ogg/Br-SaoPaulo.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>; [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language) for '[Saint Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle)') is the capital and largest city of the [state of São Paulo](229422.md). The [largest city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Brazil_by_population) in [Brazil](3383.md), it is also the [most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_cities_in_the_Americas) in [South America](26769.md), the [Americas](29833.md), and in both the [Western](73162.md) and [Southern Hemispheres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere). The city exerts international influence in commerce, finance, culture, gastronomy, arts, fashion, technology, entertainment and media, having been listed by [UNESCO](21786641.md)'s [Creative Cities Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Cities_Network) as a "[City of Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Film)" and the title of "World Capital of Gastronomy", and by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network) (GaWC) as an [alpha global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city).[^cite_note-\:1-9][^cite_note-10][^cite_note-\:2-11][^cite_note-\:4-12] It is the [largest urban area by population outside Asia](14649921.md#List) and the most populous [Portuguese-speaking city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_distribution_of_Portuguese_speakers) in the world. The city is named after [Paul the Apostle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle) and people from the capital city are known as *paulistanos* (while *paulistas* refers to people from the state of São Paulo more generally).&#x20;

Founded in 1554 by [Jesuit priests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuits), São Paulo is one of the [oldest continuously inhabited cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_continuously_inhabited_cities#South_America) on the [American continents](29833.md). It played a strategic role during the [Brazilian colonial period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_Brazil), serving as the center and starting point for the expeditions of the [Paulista *bandeirantes*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandeirantes) (pioneers) settlers for the territorial and economic expansion of the country; however, its economic opulence was consolidated during the [Brazilian coffee cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_coffee_cycle) in the mid-19th century. From that period onward, it became the stage for landmark events in [Brazilian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Brazil), such as the [Cry of Ipiranga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Brazil), the [Modern Art Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Art_Week), the [1932 Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionalist_Revolution), and the [Diretas Já](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diretas_J%C3%A1) ([Direct Elections Now](652563.md)) movement. In the 20th century, [industrialisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrialisation) consolidated its role as the main national economic hub, which made the city a [cosmopolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) [melting pot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melting_pot), home to the largest [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Brazilians), [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilians), and [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Brazilians) diasporas in the world, with [ethnic enclaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_enclave) like [Bixiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bixiga), [Bom Retiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bom_Retiro_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), and [Liberdade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberdade_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), and people from more than 200 other countries.[^cite_note-Imigrantes2016-13] The city's [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area), [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), is home to more than 20 million inhabitants and ranks as the [most populous in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_areas_in_Brazil) and one of the [most populous in the world](14649921.md#List). The process of [conurbation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conurbation) between the metropolitan areas around Greater São Paulo also created the [São Paulo Macrometropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_macrometropolis),[^cite_note-14] the first [megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalopolis) in the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere), with more than 30 million inhabitants.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-Megalópole-16]

São Paulo is the largest [urban economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_economics) in [Latin America](18524.md) and [one of the world's major financial centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index),[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] representing around 10% of the [Brazilian GDP](3634.md)[^cite_note-Brazilian_GDP-19] and just over a third of [São Paulo state's GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_state_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) (the only Brazilian city to surpass the 1 trillion [reais](34201323.md) mark in GDP).[^cite_note-20] The city is the headquarters of [B3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B3_\(stock_exchange\)), the [largest stock exchange of Latin America by market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_major_stock_exchanges),[^cite_note-B3-21] and has several [financial districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_financial_districts), mainly in the areas around [Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), [Faria Lima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadeiro_Faria_Lima_Avenue) and [Berrini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engenheiro_Lu%C3%ADs_Carlos_Berrini_Avenue) avenues. Home to 63% of established [multinationals](214491.md) in Brazil[^cite_note-Cidade_do_Mundo-22] and the source of around one third of the Brazilian scientific production,[^cite_note-23] São Paulo is among the top 50 science and technology clusters in the world.[^cite_note-24] Its main university, the [University of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), is often considered [the best in Brazil and Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_and_university_rankings),[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] while the city is regularly ranked as one of the best cities in the world to be a university student in the [QS World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QS_World_University_Rankings).[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28] The metropolis is also home to several of [the tallest skyscrapers in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Brazil), including the [Alto das Nações](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alto_das_Na%C3%A7%C3%B5es), [Platina 220](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platina_220), [Figueira Altos do Tatuapé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figueira_Altos_do_Tatuap%C3%A9), [Mirante do Vale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirante_do_Vale), [Edifício Itália](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edif%C3%ADcio_It%C3%A1lia), [Altino Arantes Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altino_Arantes_Building), [North Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centro_Empresarial_Na%C3%A7%C3%B5es_Unidas) and [many others](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). It is the [state capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_capitals_of_subdivisions_of_Brazil) with the best [basic sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_sanitation),[^cite_note-29] the second-most developed, according to the [FIRJAN Municipal Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_Federation_of_the_State_of_Rio_de_Janeiro) (2025),[^cite_note-30] the sixth in the [Social Progress Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Progress_Index) (IPS) in Brazil,[^cite_note-31] and the ninth most wooded in the country,[^cite_note-32] which earned it the international titles of "Tree City of the World" from the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN) and "[Ibero-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibero-America) Green Capital",[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] in addition to recognition from [Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records) for the "largest food security program in the world".[^cite_note-35]

The place of origin and/or disseminator of countless artistic, cultural, and gastronomic expressions, such as the [caipira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caipiras) [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caipira_culture) and cuisine, [sertanejo music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sertanejo_music), Brazilian [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_hip-hop) and [rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_rock), and [samba paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samba_paulista), and the culinary matrix of creations like [bauru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauru_\(sandwich\)), [beirute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beirute), [calabresa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingui%C3%A7a_calabresa), [coxinha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coxinha), dadinho, [parmegiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bife_%C3%A0_parmegiana), [pastel de feira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastel_\(Brazilian_food\)), and [picanha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picanha), the city is one of the main cultural, gastronomic, and entertainment hubs in the world. It is home to monuments, parks, and museums, such as the [Latin American Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_America_Memorial), [Ibirapuera Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibirapuera_Park), [São Paulo Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Art), [Pinacoteca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinacoteca_do_Estado_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Cinemateca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinemateca_Brasileira), [Itaú Cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ita%C3%BA_Cultural), [Museum of Ipiranga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museu_do_Ipiranga), [Catavento Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catavento_Museum), [Football Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Museum), [Museum of the Portuguese Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_the_Portuguese_Language), and the [Museum of Image and Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Image_and_Sound). São Paulo also holds notable cultural events like the [São Paulo Art Biennial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Art_Biennial), [São Paulo Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Fashion_Week), [Lollapalooza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lollapalooza#Brazil), [Primavera Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primavera_Sound#S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Comic Con Experience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comic_Con_Experience) and the [São Paulo Gay Pride Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Gay_Pride_Parade), the [largest LGBT event](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_LGBTQ_events) in the world.[^cite_note-36] São Paulo has also hosted many sporting events such as the [1950](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_FIFA_World_Cup) and [2014 FIFA World Cups](656933.md), the [1963 Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_Pan_American_Games), the [São Paulo Indy 300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Indy_300) and the [NFL Brazil Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NFL_International_Series#Brazil_Games), in addition to hosting the annual [Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Grand_Prix) of [Formula One](10854.md) and the [Saint Silvester Road Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Silvester_Road_Race).

## History

Main article: [History of the city of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_city_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo).

### Pre-Cabraline period

Main article: [Pre-Cabraline history of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Cabraline_history_of_Brazil)

&#x20;The first organised human settlements in the modern-day state of São Paulo is belived to have taken place in the early millennia of the [Holocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocene), between 11,000 and 9,000 years ago.[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-\:0-38][^cite_note-39] The oldest archaeological artefacts ever discovered in the municipality of São Paulo come from the Morumbi site,[^cite_note-40] theorised to be from around 3500 BC.[^cite_note-41]

Historical affiliations

> [Portugal](23033.md) [Portuguese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Empire) 1554–1815\
>  [United Kingdom of Portugal, Brazil and the Algarves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Portugal,_Brazil_and_the_Algarves) 1815–1822\
>  [Empire of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Brazil) 1822–1889\
> [Brazil](3383.md) [Republic of Brazil](3383.md) 1889–present

The region of modern-day São Paulo, originally known as Piratininga plains around the [Tietê River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River), was inhabited by the [Tupi people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupi_people), such as the [Tupiniquim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupiniquim), Guaianás, and [Guarani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaran%C3%AD_people). Other tribes also lived in areas that today form the metropolitan region.[^cite_note-42]

The region was divided in Caciquedoms (chiefdoms) at the time of encounter with the Europeans.[^cite_note-43] The most notable [cacique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cacique) was [Tibiriçá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibiri%C3%A7%C3%A1), known for his support for the Portuguese and other European colonists. Among the many indigenous place names that remain are [Tietê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Ipiranga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipiranga_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), [Tamanduateí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamanduate%C3%AD_River), [Anhangabaú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vale_do_Anhangaba%C3%BA), [Piratininga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piratininga), [Itaquaquecetuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itaquaquecetuba), [Cotia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotia), [Itapevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itapevi), [Barueri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barueri), [Embu-Guaçu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embu-Gua%C3%A7u), etc.

### Portuguese colonisation

Main article: [Portuguese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Empire)

&#x20;In 1532, [Martim Afonso de Sousa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martim_Afonso_de_Sousa) founded the first Brazilian village of [São Vicente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Vicente,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) on the coast of São Paulo. There, the first conflict between Europeans in South America took place, the [Iguape War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguape_War). *[Donatário](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donat%C3%A1rio)* of the [Captaincy of São Vicente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_of_S%C3%A3o_Vicente), Martim Afonso encouraged the occupation of the regio&#x6E;*.*[^cite_note-44]



*Founding of São Paulo*, 1909 painting by [Oscar Pereira da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Pereira_da_Silva)



The courtyard of the college, *Pátio do Colégio*, in the [Historic Center of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Center_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). At this location, the city was founded in 1554. The current building is a reconstruction made in the late 20th century, based on the [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit) [college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College) and [church](6325.md) that were erected at the site in 1653.

The Portuguese village of [São Paulo dos Campos de Piratininga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_dos_Campos_de_Piratininga) was marked by the founding of the [Colégio de São Paulo de Piratininga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%A1tio_do_Col%C3%A9gio) on 25 January 1554 founded to catechise the indigenous peoples. The [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus) college of twelve priests included [Manuel da Nóbrega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_da_N%C3%B3brega) and Spanish priest [José de Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Anchieta). They built a mission on top of a steep hill between the [Anhangabaú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anhangaba%C3%BA_River) and [Tamanduateí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamanduate%C3%AD_River) rivers.[^cite_note-Lawrence183-45][^cite_note-46]

They first had a small structure built of [rammed earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rammed_earth), made by Native Indian workers in their traditional style. The priests wanted to [evangelize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechesis) these Indians who lived in the Plateau region of Piratininga and convert them to Christianity. The site was separated from the coast by the [Serra do Mar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serra_do_Mar) mountain range, called "Serra Paranapiacaba" by the Indians.

The college was named for a Christian saint and its founding on the feast day of the celebration of the conversion of the Apostle [Paul of Tarsus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_of_Tarsus). Father José de Anchieta wrote this account in a letter to the Society of Jesus:

> The settlement of the region's Courtyard of the College began in 1560. During the visit of [Mem de Sá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mem_de_S%C3%A1), [Governor-General of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors-general_of_Brazil), the [Captaincy of São Vicente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_of_S%C3%A3o_Vicente), he ordered the transfer of the population of the Village of [São Bernardo do Campo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Bernardo_do_Campo) to the vicinity of the college. It was then named "College of St. Paul of the Piratininga". The new location was on a steep hill adjacent to a large wetland, the [Várzea do Carmo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%A1rzea_do_Carmo). It offered better protection from attacks by local Indian groups. It was renamed Vila de São Paulo, belonging to the Captaincy of São Vicente.

In 1562, troubled by the alliance between the Tupiniquim and the Portuguese, [the Tupinambás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupinamb%C3%A1_people), united and launched a series of attacks against the village on 9 July.[^cite_note-47] The defence organised by Tibiriçá and João Ramalho prevented the Tupinambás from entering São Paulo, and forced their retreat on 10 July of that year.[^cite_note-48]

On 22 March 1681, Luís Álvares de Castro, the Second Marquis de Cascais and donee of the Captaincy of [São Vicente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Vicente,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), moved the capital to the village of São Paulo *(see [Timeline of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo))*, designating it the "Head of the captaincy". The new capital was established on 23 April 1683, with public celebrations.

### Capital of the captaincy

Main article: [Bandeirantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandeirantes)

During the following two centuries, São Paulo remained a poor village isolated from the colony's centre of gravity, the coast, maintaining itself through subsistence crops. This isolation of São Paulo's isolation was mainly due to the fact that it was very difficult to climb the Serra do Mar on foot from the village of Santos or the village of São Vicente to the Piratininga Plateau. This ascent was made by the Caminho do Padre José de Anchieta. [Mem de Sá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mem_de_S%C3%A1), during his visit to the Captaincy of São Vicente, had forbidden the use of the "Caminho do Piraiquê" (today Piaçaguera), due to frequent assults by natives.

In the 17th century, men engaged in mineral exploration, the enslavement of Indigenous peoples, and the capture of runaway slaves in the southeast, then known as "paulistas" or "sertanistas," mobilised the expeditions of the bandeiras. Generally departing from São Paulo, these expeditions followed river courses, establishing trails and support points that would later develop into cities. Although they contributed to the territorial expansion of the colony of the State of Brazil, they were primarily characterised by the capture of Indigenous people for enslavement, often through attacks on Jesuit villages and missions. The principal "bandeirantes" included Fernão Dias Pais, Manuel de Borba Gato, Bartolomeu Bueno da Silva (Anhanguera), Domingos Jorge Velho, Antônio Raposo Tavares, Nicolau Barreto, and Manuel Preto.

In 1640, the Jesuits' strong opposition to the *bandeirantes*' capture and commercialisation of Indian labour led to conflicts between the two groups, culminating expulsion of the Jesuits from São Paulo until 1653.[^cite_note-49]

The discovery of gold in the region of [Minas Gerais](222651.md), in the 1690s, brought attention and new settlers to São Paulo. The Captaincy of São Paulo and Minas de Ouro (see [Captaincies of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincies_of_Brazil)) was created on 3 November 1709, when the Portuguese crown purchased the Captaincies of São Paulo and Santo Amaro from the former grantees.[^cite_note-50] Although less profitable than mines in Minas Gerais, Mato Grosso and Goiás, the search for gold in the then São Paulo de Piratininga is regarded as the first mining attempt in Portuguese South America, motivating the creation (Casa de fundição) of a Foundry House in the first century of colonisation.[^cite_note-\:3-51]

Conveniently located in the country, up the steep *[Serra do Mar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serra_do_Mar)* escarpment/mountain range when traveling from [Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santos,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), while also not too far from the coastline, São Paulo became a safe place to stay for tired travelers. The town became a center for the *[bandeirantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandeirantes)*, intrepid invaders who marched into unknown lands in search for gold, diamonds, precious stones, and Indians to enslave. The *bandeirantes*, which could be translated as "flag-bearers" or "flag-followers", mounted expeditions into the land with the primary purpose of profit and the expansion of territory for the Portuguese crown. Trade grew from the local markets and from providing food and accommodation for explorers. The *bandeirantes* eventually became politically powerful as a group, and forced the expulsion of the [Jesuits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit) from the city of São Paulo in 1640. The two groups had frequently come into conflict because of the Jesuits' opposition to the domestic [slave trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_slavery) in Indians.



The east entrance to São Paulo in 1821, by Arnaud Julien Pallière (1784–1862)

On 11 July 1711, the town of São Paulo was elevated to city status. Around the 1720s, gold was found by the pioneers in the regions near what are now [Cuiabá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuiab%C3%A1) and [Goiânia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goi%C3%A2nia). The Portuguese expanded their Brazilian territory beyond the [Tordesillas Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tordesillas_Line) to incorporate the gold regions. When the gold ran out in the late 18th century, São Paulo shifted to growing [sugar cane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_cane). Cultivation of this commodity crop spread through the interior of the Captaincy.&#x20;

The sugar was exported through the [Port of Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Santos). At that time, the first modern highway between São Paulo and the coast was constructed and named the Calçada do Lorena *("Lorena's [settway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sett_\(paving\))")*. Today the estate that is home to the Governor of the State of São Paulo, in the city of São Paulo, is called the *[Palácio dos Bandeirantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_dos_Bandeirantes)* (Bandeirantes Palace), in the [Morumbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morumbi,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) district.

### Imperial period

Main article: [Empire of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Brazil)



The [Monument to Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument_to_the_Independence_of_Brazil) in [Independence Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Park_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), located at the place where then-[Prince Pedro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_I_of_Brazil) proclaimed the [Independence of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Brazil)



[Cathedral Square of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pra%C3%A7a_da_S%C3%A9) in 1880, during the reign of [Emperor Pedro II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_II_of_Brazil) by [Marc Ferrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Ferrez_\(photographer\))

After Brazil became [independent from Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Brazil) in 1822, as declared by [Emperor Pedro I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_I_of_Brazil) where the [Monument to the Independence of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument_to_the_Independence_of_Brazil) is located, he named São Paulo as an Imperial City. In 1827, a law school was founded at the Convent of São Francisco, today part of the [University of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). The influx of students and teachers gave a new impetus to the city's growth, thanks to which the city became the *Imperial City and Borough of Students of St. Paul of Piratininga*.

The [expansion of coffee production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_cycle) was a major factor in the growth of São Paulo, as it became the region's chief export crop and yielded good revenue. It was cultivated initially in the [Paraíba Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Para%C3%ADba_Valley) region in the East of the State of São Paulo, and later on in the regions of [Campinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campinas), [Rio Claro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Claro,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [São Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Carlos) and [Ribeirão Preto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ribeir%C3%A3o_Preto).

From 1869 onward, São Paulo was connected to the port of Santos by the [Estrada de Ferro Santos-Jundiaí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estrada_de_Ferro_Santos-Jundia%C3%AD) (Santos-Jundiaí Railroad), nickname&#x64;*&#x20;The Lady*. By the late 19th century, several other railroads connected the interior to the state capital. São Paulo became the point of convergence of all railroads from the interior of the state. Coffee was the economic engine for major economic and population growth in the State of São Paulo.

In 1888, the "Golden Law" (*[Lei Áurea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lei_%C3%81urea)*) was sanctioned by [Isabel, Princess Imperial of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isabel,_Princess_Imperial_of_Brazil), abolishing [slavery in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_Brazil). Until then, enslaved had been the main source of labor in the coffee plantations. After abolition, and following governmental stimulus towards the increase of immigration, the province began to receive a large number of immigrants, largely Italians, Japanese and Portuguese peasants, many of whom settled in the capital. The region's first industries also began to emerge, providing jobs to the newcomers, especially those who had to learn Portuguese.

### Old Republican period

Main article: [Milk coffee politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milk_coffee_politics)

By the time Brazil became a republic on 15 November 1889, coffee exports were still an important part of São Paulo's economy. São Paulo grew strong in the national political scene, taking turns with the also rich state of Minas Gerais in electing [Brazilian presidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_presidents_of_Brazil), an alliance that became known as "[coffee and milk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_com_leite_politics)", given that [Minas Gerais](222651.md) was famous for its dairy production. During this period, São Paulo went from regional center to national metropolis, reaching its first million inhabitants in 1928. Its greatest growth in this period was relative in the 1890s when it doubled its population. The height of the coffee period is represented by the construction of the second [Luz Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luz_Station) (the present building) at the end of the 19th century and by the [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue) in 1900, where they built many mansions.[^cite_note-Prefeitura-52]



[Viaduto do Chá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viaduto_do_Ch%C3%A1) and [Anhangabaú Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vale_do_Anhangaba%C3%BA) in the 1920s

The [Industrial Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Revolution) created an cycle that followed the coffee plantation model. By the hands of some industrious families, including many immigrants of Italian and Jewish origin, factories began to arise and São Paulo became known for its smoky, foggy air. The cultural scene followed [modernist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernism) and [naturalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturalism_\(philosophy\)) tendencies in fashion at the beginning of the 20th century. Some examples of notable modernist artists are poets [Mário de Andrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1rio_de_Andrade) and [Oswald de Andrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oswald_de_Andrade), artists [Anita Malfatti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anita_Malfatti), [Tarsila do Amaral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarsila_do_Amaral) and [Lasar Segall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lasar_Segall), and sculptor [Victor Brecheret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Brecheret). The [Modern Art Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Art_Week) of 1922 that took place at the [Theatro Municipal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatro_Municipal_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) was an event marked by [avant-garde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avant-garde) ideas and works of art. In 1929, São Paulo won its first skyscraper, the [Martinelli Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinelli_Building).[^cite_note-Prefeitura-52]

At the beginning of the twentieth century, the valley of the Anhangabaú River was landscaped, and the region located on its left bank became known as *Centro Novo,* in the current district of the Republic. In the same period, the seat of the São Paulo government was transferred from the Pátio do Colégio to the Palácio dos Campos Elíseos, reflecting the city's expansion and modernisation movement.[^cite_note-Campos_Eliseos-53]

The modifications made in the city by Antônio da Silva Prado, Baron of Duprat and [Washington Luís](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Lu%C3%ADs), who governed from 1899 to 1919, contributed to the climate development of the city; some scholars consider that the entire city was demolished and rebuilt at that time. São Paulo's main economic activities derive from the services industry – factories are since long gone, and in came financial services institutions, law firms, consulting firms. Old factory buildings and warehouses still dot the landscape in areas such as [Barra Funda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barra_Funda) and [Brás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Br%C3%A1s).

In 1924 the city was the stage of the [São Paulo Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Revolt_of_1924), an armed conflict fought in working-class neighborhoods near central São Paulo that lasted 23 days, from 5 to 28 July, leaving hundreds dead and thousands injured. The confrontation between the federal troops of president [Artur Bernardes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artur_Bernardes) against rebels of the [Brazilian Army](1047559.md) and the [Public Force of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Forces_\(Brazil\)) was classified by the federal government as a conspiracy, a mutiny and a "revolt against the Fatherland, without foundation, headed by disorderly members of the Brazilian Army".[^cite_note-Revolução_de_1924-54] To face the rebels, the federal government launched an [indiscriminate artillery bombardment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) against the city, which affected mostly civilian targets; as a result of the bombing, a third of São Paulo's 700,000 inhabitants fled the city. The revolt has been described as "the largest [urban conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_combat_in_the_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Revolt_of_1924) in the history of Brazil".[^cite_note-55]

### 1932 revolution and contemporary era



Demonstration in [Patriarca Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarca_Square) shortly before the [Constitutionalist Revolution of 1932](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionalist_Revolution_of_1932)



São Paulo in the 1960s, with the [Martinelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinelli_Building), [Altino Arantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altino_Arantes_Building) and [Mirante do Vale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirante_do_Vale) buildings

Main article: [Constitutionalist Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionalist_Revolution)

In 1932, São Paulo became the centre of the [Constitutionalist Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionalist_Revolution), when the entire population engaged in the war against the "Provisional Government" of [Getúlio Vargas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Get%C3%BAlio_Vargas). In 1934, with the reunion of some faculties created in the 19th century, the [University of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) (USP) was founded, today the largest in Brazil.[^cite_note-HCSP-56][^cite_note-Times-57]

The first major project for industrial installation in the city was the industrial complex of [Indústrias Matarazzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ind%C3%BAstrias_Reunidas_F%C3%A1bricas_Matarazzo) in [Barra Funda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barra_Funda). In the 1930s, the Jafet brothers, operating in the fabric business, Rodolfo Crespi, the Puglisi Carbone brothers and the Klabin family, who would found the first large cellulose industry in Brazil, the [Klabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klabin).[^cite_note-58] Another major industrial boom occurred during the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War), due to the crisis in coffee farming in the 1930s and restrictions on international trade during the war, which resulted in the city having a very high economic growth rate that remained high in the post-war period.[^cite_note-SP450-59]

In 1947, São Paulo gained its first paved highway: the [Via Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Via_Anchieta) (built on the old route of [José de Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Anchieta)), connecting the capital to the coast of São Paulo. In the 1950s, São Paulo was known as "the city that never stop" and as "the fastest growing city in the world".[^cite_note-SP450-59] São Paulo held a large celebration, in 1954, of the "Fourth Centenary" of the city's founding, when the [Ibirapuera Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibirapuera_Park) was inaugurated, many historical books are released and the source of the [Tietê River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River) in [Salesópolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales%C3%B3polis) is discovered. With the transfer of part of the city's financial center, which was located in the historic center (in the region called the "Historic Triangle"), to [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), its mansions were, for the most part, replaced by large buildings.[^cite_note-SP450-59]



Prestes Maia Avenue in 1974

From the 1930s to the 1960s, the great entrepreneurs of São Paulo's development were mayor [Francisco Prestes Maia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Prestes_Maia) and the governor [Ademar de Barros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ademar_de_Barros), who was also mayor of São Paulo between 1957 and 1961. Prestes Maia designed and implemented, in the 1930s, the "Avenue Plan for the City of São Paulo", which overhauled São Paulo's traffic.[^cite_note-60] These two rulers are also responsible for the two biggest urban interventions, after the Avenues Plan, which changed São Paulo: the [rectification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_engineering) of the Tietê river with the construction of its banks and the [São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro): on February 13, 1963, governor Ademar de Barros and mayor Prestes Maia created study commissions (state and municipal) to prepare the basic project for the São Paulo Metro, and allocated their first funds to the Metro.[^cite_note-61]

At the beginning of the 1960s, São Paulo had four million inhabitants. Construction of the metro began in 1968, under the administration of Mayor [José Vicente de Faria Lima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Vicente_de_Faria_Lima), and the commercial operation started on September 14, 1974. In 2016 the system had a network 71.5 km long and 64 stations spread across five lines. That year, 1.1 billion passengers were transported by the system.[^cite_note-MetroHist-62]



Doctors stabilising a COVID-19 patient in critical condition in Vila Nova Cachoeirinha

At the end of the 20th century and beginning of the 21st century, São Paulo became the main financial hub in [South America](26769.md) and one of the most populous cities in the world. As the most influential Brazilian city on the global stage, São Paulo is currently classified as an [alpha global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network#Alpha).[^cite_note-GaWC-63] The metropolis has [one of the largest GDP in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_GDP), representing, alone, 11% of all [Brazilian GDP](3634.md),[^cite_note-Brazilian_GDP-19] and is also responsible for one third of the Brazilian scientific production.[^cite_note-Unicamp_2005-64]



[Skyline of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) in 2024 from [Aclimação](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aclima%C3%A7%C3%A3o). In the background, the buildings of [Avenida Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenida_Paulista), as well as [Pico do Jaraguá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pico_do_Jaragu%C3%A1) to the right of the photograph.

## Geography



[Jaraguá Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pico_do_Jaragu%C3%A1) is the highest point in the city, at 1,135 meters (3,724 ft).

[^cite_note-65]

São Paulo is the capital of the most populous state in Brazil, [São Paulo](229422.md), located at [latitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latitude) 23°33'01'' south and [longitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longitude) 46°38'02'' west. The total area of the municipality is 1,521.11 square kilometres (587.30 sq mi), according to the [Brazilian Institute of Geography and Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Institute_of_Geography_and_Statistics) (IBGE), being the ninth largest in the state in terms of territorial extension.[^cite_note-IBGE_SãoPaulo-66] Of the entire area of the municipality, 949,611 square kilometres (366,647 sq mi) are urban areas (2015), being the largest urban area in the country.[^cite_note-EMBRAPA-2015-67] The urban organisation, as in the whole colony, was centred, administratively and ecclesiastically, in [parishes](23623.md). Each parish was centred on a chapel.[^cite_note-\:5-68]

The city is on a [plateau](538005.md) placed beyond the [Serra do Mar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serra_do_Mar) (Portuguese for "Sea Range" or "Coastal Range"), itself a component of the vast region known as the [Brazilian Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Highlands), with an average elevation of around 799 meters (2,621 ft) [above sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level), although being at a distance of only about 70 km (43 mi) from [the Atlantic Ocean](698.md). The distance is covered by two highways, the [Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Anchieta) and the [Imigrantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_dos_Imigrantes), (see "[Transportation](#Transportation)" below) that roll down the range, leading to the port city of [Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santos,_Brazil) and the beach resort of [Guarujá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaruj%C3%A1). Rolling terrain prevails within the urbanized areas of São Paulo except in its northern area, where the [Serra da Cantareira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serra_da_Cantareira) Range reaches a higher elevation and a sizable remnant of the [Atlantic Rain Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Forest). The region is seismically stable and no significant [activity](10106.md) has ever been recorded.[^cite_note-69]

### Hydrography



[Tietê River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River), with the [Marginal Tietê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marginal_Tiet%C3%AA) highway



[Billings Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billings_Reservoir)

See also: [Water management in the Metropolitan Region of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_management_in_the_Metropolitan_Region_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

The [Tietê River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River) and its [tributary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tributary), the [Pinheiros River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinheiros_River), were once important sources of [fresh water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshwater) and leisure for São Paulo. However, heavy industrial [effluents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effluents) and [wastewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wastewater) discharges in the later 20th century caused the rivers to become [heavily polluted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_pollution). A substantial clean-up program for both rivers is underway.[^cite_note-veja.abril.com.br-70][^cite_note-www1.folha.uol.com.br-71] Neither river is navigable in the stretch that flows through the city, although [water transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_transportation) becomes increasingly important on the Tietê river further downstream (near [river Paraná](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paran%C3%A1_River)), as the river is part of the [River Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%ADo_de_la_Plata) basin.[^cite_note-72]

No large natural lakes exist in the region, but the [Billings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billings_Reservoir) and [Guarapiranga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guarapiranga) reservoirs in the city's southern outskirts are used for [power generation](9540.md), water storage and leisure activities, such as sailing. The original [flora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora) consisted mainly of [broadleaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flowering_plant) [evergreens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evergreen). [Non-native species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Introduced_species) are common, as the mild climate and abundant rainfall permit a multitude of subtropical and temperate plants to be cultivated, especially the ubiquitous [eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus).[^cite_note-73]

The north of the municipality contains part of the 7,917 hectares (19,560 acres) [Cantareira State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantareira_State_Park), created in 1962, which protects a large part of the metropolitan São Paulo water supply.[^cite_note-74] In 2015, São Paulo experienced a [major drought](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Brazilian_drought), which led several cities in the state to start a rationing system.[^cite_note-nyt2.2015-75]

### Parks and biodiversity



[Cantareira State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantareira_State_Park), part of the [São Paulo Green Belt Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_belt#Brazil)



[São Paulo Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Zoo) in the [Fontes do Ipiranga Biological Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fontes_do_Ipiranga_Biological_Reserve)

São Paulo is located in an [ecotone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecotone) area between 3 [biomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biome): [mixed ombrophilous forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Araucaria_moist_forests), dense ombrophilous forest and [cerrado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerrado); the latter had some plant species native to the [pampas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pampas) in the city. There were several species typical of both biomes, among them we can mention: [araucarias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Araucaria), [pitangueiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenia_uniflora), [cambucís](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campomanesia_phaea), [ipês](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handroanthus), [jabuticabeiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campomanesia_phaea), [queen palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syagrus_romanzoffiana), [muricís-do-campo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrsonima_basiloba), etc.[^cite_note-76]

In 2010, São Paulo had 62 municipal and state parks,[^cite_note-77] such as the [Cantareira State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantareira_State_Park), part of the [São Paulo Green Belt Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_belt#Brazil) and home to one of the largest [urban forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_forest) on the planet with 7,900 hectares (20,000 acres) of extension,[^cite_note-78] the Fontes do Ipiranga State Park, the [Ibirapuera Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibirapuera_Park), the Tietê Ecological Park, the Capivari-Monos Environmental Protection Area, the [Serra do Mar State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serra_do_Mar_State_Park), [Villa-Lobos State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa-Lobos_State_Park), [People's Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Park_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), and the [Jaraguá State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaragu%C3%A1_State_Park), listed as a [World Heritage Site](44940.md) by [UNESCO](21786641.md) in 1994.[^cite_note-79]

In 2009, São Paulo had 2,300 hectares (5,700 acres) of green area, less than 1.5% of the city's area[^cite_note-80] and below the 12 square metres (130 sq ft) per inhabitant recommended by the [WHO](33583.md).[^cite_note-81] About 21% of the municipality's area is covered by green areas, including ecological reserves (2010 data).[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83]

In the municipality it is possible to observe forest birds that usually appear in the spring, due to the belt of native forest that still surrounds the metropolitan region. Species such as the [rufous-bellied thrush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rufous-bellied_thrush), [golden-chevroned tanager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden-chevroned_tanager), [great kiskadee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_kiskadee) and hummingbird are the most common. Despite the intense pollution, the main rivers of the city, the Tietê and the Pinheiros, shelter several species of animals such as [capybaras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capybara), [hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawk), [southern lapwings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_lapwing), [herons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heron) and [nutrias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nutria). Other species found in the municipality are the [gray brocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_brocket), [howler monkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howler_monkey), [green-billed toucan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green-billed_toucan) and the [Amazonian umbrellabird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazonian_umbrellabird).[^cite_note-84]

### Environment



[Tietê River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River), one of the most polluted in the city



[Smog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smog) seen on the horizon of the Ibirapuera region

[Air pollution](10934212.md) in some districts of the city exceeds local standards, mainly due to car traffic.[^cite_note-Ribeiro-85] The [WHO](33583.md) sets a limit of 20 micrograms of particulate matter per cubic meter of air as a safe annual average. In an assessment carried out by the WHO among over a thousand cities around the world in 2011, the city of São Paulo was ranked 268th among the most polluted, with an average rate of 38 micrograms per cubic meter, a rate well above the WHO-imposed limit, but lower than in other Brazilian cities, such as [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md) (64 micrograms per cubic meter).[^cite_note-86] A 2013 study found that air pollution in the city causes more deaths than [traffic accidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_accidents).[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88]

The stretch of the [Tietê River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River) that runs through the city is the most polluted river in Brazil.[^cite_note-89] In 1992, the Tietê Project began, with the aim to clean up the river by 2005. 8.8 billion reais was spent on the failed project.[^cite_note-BBC-90] In 2019, the Novo Rio Pinheiros Project began, under the administration of [João Doria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Doria), with the aim to reduce sewage discharged into the Tietê's tributary, the [Pinheiros River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinheiros_River).[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92]

The problem of balanced water supply for the city – and for the metropolis, in general – is also a worrying issue: São Paulo has few sources of water in its own perimeter, having to seek it in distant hydrographic basins. The problem of water pollution is also aggravated by the irregular occupation of watershed areas, caused by urban expansion, driven by the difficulty of access to land and housing in central areas by the low-income population[^cite_note-93] and associated with real estate speculation and precariousness in new subdivisions. With this, there is also an overvaluation of individual transport over public transport – leading to the current rate of more than one vehicle for every two inhabitants and aggravating the problem of environmental pollution.[^cite_note-94]

### Climate



Heavy rain and lightning in São Paulo, which has one of the largest number of [lightning](61344.md) incidents amongst Brazilian state capitals

[^cite_note-95]

The climate of São Paulo is characterised as [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *Cfa*, bordering on *Cwa*).[^cite_note-Climate-96] In summer (January through March), the mean low temperature is about 19 °C (66 °F) and the mean high temperature is near 28 °C (82 °F). In winter, temperatures tend to range between 12 and 22 °C (54 and 72 °F). The record high temperature was 38.4 °C (101.1 °F) on 17 October 2014[^cite_note-bdmep.inmet.gov.br-97] and the lowest −3.2 °C (26.2 °F) on 25 June 1918.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99] The highest accumulated rainfall in 24 hours was 151.8 mm (5.98 in) on December 21, 1988, followed by 144.1 mm (5.67 in) on January 25, 2025, and 140.4 mm (5.53 in) on May 25, 2005.[^cite_note-bdmep.inmet.gov.br-97]

The [Tropic of Capricorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropic_of_Capricorn), at about 23°27' S, passes through north of São Paulo and roughly marks the boundary between the tropical and [temperate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temperateness) areas of South America. Because of its elevation, São Paulo has a more temperate climate.[^cite_note-100] The summer is hot and rainy. Autumn and spring are transitional seasons. Winter is mild, but still the coldest season, with cloudiness around town and frequent polar air masses. [Frosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frost) occur sporadically in regions further away from the center, in some winters throughout the city.[^cite_note-101]

Rainfall is abundant, annually averaging 1,658 millimeters (65.3 in). It is especially common in the warmer months averaging 292 millimeters (11.5 in) in January and decreases in winter, averaging 32 millimeters (1.3 in).[^cite_note-INMETprecip-102] Neither São Paulo nor the nearby coast has ever been hit by a [tropical cyclone](8282374.md) and [tornadic](37530.md) activity is uncommon. During late winter, especially August, the city experiences the phenomenon known as *"veranico"* or *"verãozinho"* ("little summer"), which consists of hot and dry weather, sometimes reaching temperatures well above 28 °C (82 °F).&#x20;

&#x20;Relatively cool summer days are fairly common when persistent winds blow from the ocean, with daily highs sometimes remaining below 20 °C (68 °F) and lows often falling below 15 °C (59 °F). Conversely, spring and summer can become extremely hot during heat waves, with temperatures reaching around 34 °C (93 °F). In areas with a higher density of skyscrapers and less tree cover, such as [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), temperatures can feel as high as 39 °C (102 °F). During the summer of 2014, São Paulo experienced a heat wave lasting nearly four weeks, with temperatures exceeding 30 °C (86 °F) and peaking at 36 °C (97 °F). Driven in part by [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation), [groundwater pollution](44413707.md), and [climate change](5042951.md), the city is increasingly susceptible to [drought](37335.md) and [water scarcity](15380061.md).[^cite_note-103]

## Demographics

See also: [Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilians) and [Demographics of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Brazil)

| Year | Pop. | ±% |
| --- | --- | --- |

| 1890 | 64,934 | +106.9% |
| 1900 | 239,820 | +269.3% |
| 1920 | 579,033 | +141.4% |
| 1940 | 1,326,261 | +129.0% |
| 1950 | 2,198,096 | +65.7% |

São Paulo's population has grown rapidly. In 1960, it surpassed Rio de Janeiro as the most populous city in Brazil.[^cite_note-123] By that time, the Greater São Paulo area had extended beyond the boundaries of the municipality proper into surrounding municipalities, making it a metropolitan area with a population of 4.6 million. Population growth has continued since 1960, although the rate of growth has slowed.[^cite_note-124]

In 2013, São Paulo was the most populous city in Brazil and in South America.[^cite_note-125] According to the [2010 IBGE Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Institute_of_Geography_and_Statistics), there were 11,244,369 people residing in the city of São Paulo.[^cite_note-126] Portuguese remains the most widely spoken language and São Paulo is the largest city in the [Portuguese speaking world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_speaking_world).[^cite_note-127]

In 2010, the city had 2,146,077 opposite-sex couples and 7,532 [same-sex couples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_couple). The population of São Paulo was 52.6% female and 47.4% male.[^cite_note-censo2010.ibge.gov.br-128] The [2022 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_census) found 6,214,422 [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Brazilian) people (54.3%), 3,820,326 [Pardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pardo_Brazilians) ([multiracial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial#Brazil)) people (33.4%), 1,160,073 [Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Brazilian) people (10.1%), 238,603 [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Brazilian) people (2.1%) and 17,727 [Amerindian](55510.md) people (0.2%).[^cite_note-129]

### Immigration and migration

Main article: [Immigration to Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Brazil)

| Ethnicity |  | Percentage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Brazilian) |  | 54.3% |
| [Mixed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pardo) |  | 33.4% |
| [Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Brazilian) |  | 10.1% |
| [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Brazilian) |  | 2.1% |
| [Amerindian](55510.md) |  | 0.2% |

São Paulo is considered the most [multicultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multicultural) city in Brazil. From 1870 to 2010, approximately 2.3 million [immigrants](4599312.md) arrived in the state, from all parts of the world. The [Italian community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilians) is one of the strongest, with a presence throughout the city. Of the 12 million inhabitants of São Paulo, 50% (5 million people) have full or partial Italian ancestry. São Paulo has more [descendants of Italians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilians) than any Italian city (the largest city of [Italy](14532.md) is [Rome](25458.md), with 2.8 million inhabitants).[^cite_note-130]

The main groups, considering all the metropolitan area, are: 6 million people of [Portuguese descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Brazilian),[^cite_note-131] 5 million people of [Italian descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilian),[^cite_note-132] 1.7 million people of [African descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Brazilian),[^cite_note-133] 1 million people of [Arab descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Brazilian),[^cite_note-autogenerated1-134] 665,000 people of [Japanese descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Brazilian),[^cite_note-autogenerated1-134] 400,000 people of [German descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Brazilian),[^cite_note-autogenerated1-134] 250,000 people of [French descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Brazilian),[^cite_note-autogenerated1-134] 150,000 people of [Greek descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Brazilian),[^cite_note-autogenerated1-134] 120,000 people of [Chinese descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Brazilian),[^cite_note-autogenerated1-134] 120,000–300,000 [Bolivian immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivians_in_Brazil),[^cite_note-135] 50,000 people of [Korean descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Brazilian),[^cite_note-136] and 80,000 [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_Brazil).[^cite_note-137]



[Italian immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilian) in the [Immigrant Inn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigrant_Inn), c. 1890



The [Liberdade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberdade_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) district is a [Japantown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japantown) of São Paulo.



[Casa de Portugal in São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_Portugal_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) headquarters



Photos of [Jewish immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Brazil) at the Jewish Immigration Memorial in [Bom Retiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bom_Retiro_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\))

Even today, Italians are grouped in areas like [Bixiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bixiga), [Brás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Br%C3%A1s), and [Mooca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mooca_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) to promote celebrations and festivals. In the early twentieth century, Italian and its dialects were spoken almost as much as [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language) in the city, which influenced the formation of the São Paulo dialect of today. Six thousand pizzerias are producing about a million pizzas a day. Brazil has the largest [Italian population](385155.md) outside [Italy](14532.md), with São Paulo being the most populous city with Italian ancestry in the world.[^cite_note-138]

The [Portuguese community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Brazilians) is the largest, consisting of 6 million inhabitants. From the beginning, the Portuguese formed the root and foundation of Brazil, as they installed the Portuguese language, culture, the Catholic religion, laws, government bodies, courts, educational institutions, etc, in Brazil. Paulistanos have considerable origin in [Portugal](23033.md). The [Jewish colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Brazil) is more than 80,000 people in São Paulo and is concentrated mainly in [Higienópolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higien%C3%B3polis,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) and [Bom Retiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bom_Retiro_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)).[^cite_note-forbesimi-139]

From the nineteenth century through the first half of the twentieth century, São Paulo also received [German immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Brazilians) (in the current [Santo Amaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santo_Amaro,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) district), [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_people) and [Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_people) (in the neighborhood [Vila Zelina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vila_Zelina)).[^cite_note-forbesimi-139]

> "A French observer, travelling to São Paulo at the time, noted that there was a division of the capitalist class, by nationality (...) Germans, French and Italians shared the dry goods sector with Brazilians. Foodstuffs was generally the province of either Portuguese or Brazilians, except for bakery and pastry which was the domain of the French and Germans. Shoes and tinware were mostly controlled by Italians. However, the larger metallurgical plants were in the hands of the English and the Americans. (...) Italians outnumbered Brazilians two to one in São Paulo."

— [^cite_note-140]

Until 1920, 1,078,437 [Italians](385155.md) entered in the [State of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). Between 1888 and 1919, 38% of the immigrants were Italians, 10.2% were [Spaniards](963152.md) and 40% were [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_people).[^cite_note-100_anos_de_imigração-141] In 1920, nearly 80% of São Paulo city's population was composed of immigrants and their descendants and Italians made up over half of its male population.[^cite_note-100_anos_de_imigração-141] São Paulo also is home of the largest [Japanese community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_diaspora) outside [Japan](15573.md).[^cite_note-142] In 1958 the census counted 120,000 Japanese in the city and by 1987, there were 326,000 with another 170,000 in the surrounding areas within São Paulo state.[^cite_note-Lesserp3-143] As of 2007, the [Paulistano Japanese population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_community_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) outnumbered their fellow diaspora in [the entirety of Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Peruvian), and in all individual American cities.[^cite_note-Lesserp3-143]

Research conducted by the [University of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) (USP) shows the city's high ethnic diversity: when asked if they are "descendants of foreign immigrants", 81% of the students reported "yes". The main reported ancestries were: Italian (30.5%), Portuguese (35%), Spanish (14%), Japanese (8%), German (6%), *Brazilian* (4%), African (3%), Arab (2%) and Jewish (1%).[^cite_note-144]

The city once attracted numerous immigrants from all over Brazil and even from foreign countries, due to a strong economy and for being the hub of most Brazilian companies.[^cite_note-145] São Paulo is also receiving waves of immigration from [Haiti](13373.md) and from many countries of [Africa](5334607.md) and the [Caribbean](18956035.md). Those immigrants are mainly concentrated in [Praça da Sé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pra%C3%A7a_da_S%C3%A9), [Glicério](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glic%C3%A9rio) and [Vale do Anhangabaú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vale_do_Anhangaba%C3%BA) in the [Central Zone of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Zone_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo).

Since the 19th century people began migrating from [northeastern Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Region,_Brazil) into São Paulo. This migration grew enormously in the 1930s and remained huge in the next decades.[^cite_note-146]

### Metropolitan area

Main articles: [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) and [São Paulo Macrometropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Macrometropolis)



Satellite view of [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) at night

The nonspecific term "Grande São Paulo" ("*[Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)*") covers multiple definitions. The legally defined *Região Metropolitana de São Paulo* consists of 39 municipalities in total and a population of 21.1 million[^cite_note-bra-147] inhabitants (as of the 2014 National Census).

Because São Paulo has [urban sprawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_sprawl), it uses a different definition for its metropolitan area alternately called the Expanded Metropolitan Complex of São Paulo and the [São Paulo Macrometropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Macrometropolis). Analogous to the [BosWash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_megalopolis) definition, it is one of the largest urban agglomerations in the world, with 32 million inhabitants,[^cite_note-148] behind [Tokyo](354951.md), which includes four contiguous legally defined metropolitan regions and three micro-regions.
