See also: [Religion in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Brazil), [Protestantism in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_Brazil), and [Roman Catholic Church in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church_in_Brazil)



[São Paulo Metropolitan Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metropolitan_Cathedral)



[Orthodox Cathedral of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Cathedral_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[Temple of Solomon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Solomon_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) of the [UCKG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Church_of_the_Kingdom_of_God)



[Buddhist](3267529.md) Lohan Temple, [Liberdade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberdade_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\))



[Brás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Br%C3%A1s) mosque



[Beth El Synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beth_El_Synagogue_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\))

The [official religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion) of the crown, the Portugeese colonisers brought with them Catholicism, which the majority of the population remain today. In 1891, when the first [Brazilian Republican Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Constitution_of_1891) was set forth, Brazil ceased to have an official religion and has remained secular ever since, though the Catholic Church remained politically influential into the 1970s. The constitution of Brazil guarantees [freedom of religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Brazil) and strongly prohibits the [establishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion) of any religion by banning government support or hindrance of religion at all levels.[^cite_note-georgetown1-150]

According to data from the [Brazilian Institute of Geography and Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Institute_of_Geography_and_Statistics) (IBGE), in 2010 the population of São Paulo was 6,549,775 [Roman Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholics) (58.2%), 2,887,810 [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestants) (22.1%), 531,822 [Spiritists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritists) (4.7%), 101,493 [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses) (0.9%), 75,075 [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhists) (0.7%), 50,794 [Umbandists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbanda) (0.5%), 43,610 [Jews](25955086.md) (0.4%), 28,673 [Catholic Apostolic Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Catholic_Apostolic_Church) (0.3%), 25,583 eastern religious (0.2%), 18,058 [Candomblecists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candombl%C3%A9) (0.2%), 17,321 [Mormons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormons) (0.2%), 14,894 Eastern Orthodox (0.1%), 9,119 [spiritualists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritualism_\(movement\)) (0.1%), 8,277 [Muslims](19541.md) (0.1%), 7,139 [esoteric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_esotericism) (0.1%), 1,829 practiced Indian traditions (<0.1%) and 1,008 were [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu) (<0.1%). Others 1,056 008 had no religion (9.4%), 149,628 followed other Christian religiosities (1.3%), 55,978 had an undetermined religion or multiple belonging (0.5%), 14,127 did not know (0.1%) And 1,896 reported following other religiosities (<0.1%).[^cite_note-Religion-149]

The Catholic Church divides the territory of the municipality of São Paulo into four ecclesiastical circumscriptions: the [Archdiocese of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), and the adjacent Diocese of Santo Amaro, the Diocese of São Miguel Paulista and the Diocese of Campo Limpo, the last three suffragans of the first. The archive of the archdiocese, called the Metropolitan Archival Dom Duarte Leopoldo e Silva, in the [Ipiranga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipiranga_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) district, holds one of the most important documentary heritage in Brazil. The archiepiscopal is the [Metropolitan Cathedral of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Cathedral) (known as Sé Cathedral), in [Praça da Sé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pra%C3%A7a_da_S%C3%A9), considered one of the five largest [Gothic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_Revival_architecture) temples in the world. The Catholic Church recognizes as patron saints of the city [Saint Paul of Tarsus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle) and [Our Lady of Penha of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Pe%C3%B1afrancia).[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-IBGE_2010-152]

The city has the most diverse Protestant or Reformed creeds, such as the Evangelical Community of Our Land, Maranatha Christian Church, [Lutheran Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_Church), [Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church), [Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist_Church), [Anglican Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Episcopal_Church), [Baptist churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist_church), [Assemblies of God in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembleias_de_Deus) (the largest evangelical church in the country),[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154] The [Seventh-day Adventist Church](28632.md), the World Church of God's Power, the Universal Church of the Kingdom of God, the [Christian Congregation in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Congregation_in_Brazil),[^cite_note-155] among others, as well as Christians of various denominations.[^cite_note-IBGE_2010-152]

### Public security



[Tactical operations center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tactical_operations_center) of the [Military Police of São Paulo State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Police_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_State) in São Paulo



Training of soldiers of the [Military Police of São Paulo State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Police_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_State) at the Military Police Academy of Barro Branco

In 2008, the city of São Paulo ranked 493rd on the list of the most violent cities in Brazil. Among the capitals, it was the fourth least violent, registering, in 2006, homicide rates higher only than those of [Boa Vista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boa_Vista,_Roraima), [Palmas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmas,_Tocantins) and [Natal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natal,_Rio_Grande_do_Norte).[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] In November 2009, the Ministry of Justice and the Brazilian Public Security Forum released a survey that identified São Paulo as the safest Brazilian capital for young people.[^cite_note-158] Between 2000 and 2010, the city of São Paulo reduced its homicide rate by 78%.[^cite_note-159]

According to the 2011 Global Homicide Study, released by the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN), in the period between 2004 and 2009 the homicide rate fell from 20.8 to 10.8 murders per hundred thousand inhabitants. In 2011, the UN pointed to São Paulo as an example of how large cities can reduce crime.[^cite_note-160] In a survey on the Adolescent Homicide Index (AHI) in 2010, the city of São Paulo was considered the least lethal for adolescents, among 283 municipalities surveyed, with more than 100,000 inhabitants.[^cite_note-161] According to data from the "Map of Violence 2011", published by the Sangari Institute and the Ministry of Justice, the city had the lowest homicide rate per hundred thousand inhabitants that year among all the state capitals in Brazil.[^cite_note-Mapa_da_violência-162]

Crime indicators, such as homicide, according to data from April 2017, showed a reduction in the capital of São Paulo, compared to 2016. In the same period, there was a 12.64% reduction in homicides, the number of robbery records fell by eleven to seven (34% reduction), and there was an 8.09% reduction in rape cases.[^cite_note-163] The 9th DP in the Carandiru neighborhood was considered, in March 2007, one of the five best police stations in the world and the best in [Latin America](18524.md).[^cite_note-164]

Based on data from IBGE and the Ministry of Health, it is considered the 2nd safest capital[^cite_note-165] and the least lethal capital in the country, according to the 2023 Brazilian Public Security Yearbook.[^cite_note-166]

### Social challenges

Main article: [Social issues in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_issues_in_Brazil)



[Paraisópolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parais%C3%B3polis_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), the largest [slum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slum) (*[favela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Favela)*) in the city, with residential buildings in the background



[Public housing](177912.md) in [Heliópolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heli%C3%B3polis)

Since the beginning of the 20th century, São Paulo has been a major economic focal point in Latin America. During two [World Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_war) and the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression), coffee exports (from other regions of the state) were critically affected. This led wealthy coffee farmers to invest in industrial activities that turned São Paulo into Brazil's largest industrial hub.

- [Crime rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_statistics) consistently decreased in the 21st century. The citywide homicide rate was 6.56 in 2019, less than a fourth of the 27.38 national rate.

  [^cite_note-167]

- Air quality

  [^cite_note-Ribeiro-85]

  &#x20;has steadily increased during the modern era.

- The two major rivers crossing the city, [Tietê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_River) and [Pinheiros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinheiros_River), are highly polluted. A major project to clean up these rivers is underway.

  [^cite_note-veja.abril.com.br-70]

  [^cite_note-www1.folha.uol.com.br-71]

- The [Clean City Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cidade_Limpa) or anti[billboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard), approved in 2007, focused on two main targets: anti-publicity and anti-commerce. Advertisers estimate that they removed 15,000 billboards and that more than 1,600 [signs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signage) and 1,300 towering metal panels were dismantled by authorities.

  [^cite_note-168]

- São Paulo metropolitan region, adopted vehicle restrictions from 1996 to 1998 to reduce air pollution during wintertime. Since 1997, a similar project was implemented throughout the year in the central area of São Paulo to improve traffic.

  [^cite_note-169]

- There were more than 30,000 homeless people in 2021 according to official data. It increased by 31% in two years, and doubled in 20 years.

  [^cite_note-170]

### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Brazil)

The primary language is [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language). The general language from São Paulo General, or [Tupi Austral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupi_language) (Southern Tupi), was the Tupi-based trade language of what is now São Vicente, São Paulo, and the upper Tietê River. In the 17th century it was widely spoken in São Paulo and spread to its border regions while in Brazil. From 1750 on, following orders from [Marquess of Pombal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquess_of_Pombal), Portuguese language was introduced through immigration and consequently taught to children in schools. The original [Tupi Austral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupi_Austral) language subsequently lost ground to [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language), and eventually became extinct. Due to the large influx of [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Brazilians), [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Brazilians), [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilians_of_Spanish_descent), [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilian) and [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Brazilian) immigrants etc., the Portuguese idiom spoken in the metropolitan area of São Paulo reflects influences from those languages.



[Museum of the Portuguese Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_the_Portuguese_Language) interior

The Italian influence in São Paulo accents is evident in its Italian districts such as Bela Vista, [Mooca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mooca_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), [Brás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Br%C3%A1s) and Lapa. Italian mingled with Portuguese and as an old influence, was assimilated or disappeared into spoken language. The local accent with Italian influences became notorious through the songs of [Adoniran Barbosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoniran_Barbosa), a Brazilian [samba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samba) singer born to Italian parents who used to sing using the local accent.[^cite_note-171]

Other languages spoken in the city are mainly among the Asian community: São Paulo is home to the largest Japanese population outside Japan. Although today most Japanese-Brazilians speak only Portuguese, some of them are still fluent in Japanese. Some people of Chinese and Korean descent are still able to speak their ancestral languages.[^cite_note-172] In some areas it is still possible to find descendants of immigrants who speak German[^cite_note-173] (especially in the area of Brooklin paulista) and Lithuanian or Russian or East European languages (especially in the area of Vila Zelina).[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-BBC_Brasil-175] In the west zone of São Paulo, specially at Vila Anastácio and Lapa region, there is a [Hungarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_people) colony, with three churches (Calvinist, Baptist and Catholic), so on Sundays it is possible to see Hungarians talking to each other on sidewalks.

### Sexual diversity

Main articles: [LGBT rights in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Brazil), [LGBT people in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_people_in_Brazil), [LGBT history in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_history_in_Brazil), and [Same-sex marriage in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Brazil)



The 18th edition of the [São Paulo Gay Pride Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Gay_Pride_Parade) in 2014

The [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) is home to a prominent self-identifying [gay](5171128.md), [bisexual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisexual) and [transgender](19904525.md) community, with 9.6% of the male population and 7% of the female population declaring themselves to be non-heterosexual.[^cite_note-176] Same-sex [civil unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union) were legalised in Brazil in 2011 and [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Brazil) in 2012. Since 1997, the city has hosted the annual [São Paulo Gay Pride Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Gay_Pride_Parade), considered the biggest [pride parade](58168.md) in the world by the [Guinness Book of World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_Book_of_World_Records) with over 5 million participants, and typically rivalling the [New York City Pride March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Pride_March) for the record.[^cite_note-NYCWorld'sLargestPrideParade-177]

Strongly supported by the State and the City of São Paulo government authorities, in 2010, the city hall of São Paulo invested R\$1 million reais in the parade and provided a solid security plan, with approximately 2,000 policemen, two mobile police stations for immediate reporting of occurrences, 30 equipped ambulances, 55 nurses, 46 medical physicians, three hospital camps with 80 beds. The parade, considered the city's second largest event after the [Formula One](10854.md), begins at the [São Paulo Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Art), crosses [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), and follows Consolação Street to [Praça Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pra%C3%A7a_Roosevelt) in [Downtown São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). According to the LGBT app [Grindr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grindr), the gay parade of the city was elected the best in the world.[^cite_note-178]

### Education

Main articles: [Education in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Brazil) and [Brazilian science and technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_science_and_technology)



[University of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[St Paul's School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Paul's_School,_Brazil), [British-Brazilian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Brazilians) school ranked by [The Schools Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Schools_Index) as one of the 150 best private schools in the world, and among the top 5 schools in [Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%ADvia_Andrade).

[^cite_note-179]

São Paulo has public and private primary and secondary schools and vocational-[technical schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technical_school). More than nine-tenths of the population are literate and roughly the same proportion of those age 7 to 14 are enrolled in school. There are 578 universities in the state of São Paulo.[^cite_note-180]

The city of São Paulo is also home to research and development facilities and attracts companies due to the presence of regionally renowned universities. Science, technology and innovation is leveraged by the allocation of funds from the [state government](229422.md), mainly carried out by means of the Foundation to Research Support in the State of São Paulo (Fundação de Amparo à Pesquisa do Estado de São Paulo – FAPESP), one of the main agencies promoting scientific and technological research.[^cite_note-181]

Further information: [Faculdade de Informática e Administração Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculdade_de_Inform%C3%A1tica_e_Administra%C3%A7%C3%A3o_Paulista)

### Health care

Main article: [Health in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Brazil)

See also: [Rede São Paulo Saudável](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rede_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Saud%C3%A1vel)



[Institute of Cancer of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Cancer_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), the largest [oncology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oncology) center in [Latin America](18524.md)

[^cite_note-182]

[^cite_note-183]

São Paulo is one of the largest health care hubs in Latin America. Among its hospitals are the [Albert Einstein Israelite Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Einstein_Israelite_Hospital), ranked the best hospital in all Latin America[^cite_note-184] and the [Hospital das Clínicas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospital_das_Cl%C3%ADnicas_da_Universidade_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), the largest in the region, with a total area of 600,000 square meters and offers 2,400 beds, distributed among its eight specialized institutes and two assisting hospitals.[^cite_note-185]

The main hospitals in the city of São Paulo concentrate in the upper-income areas, the majority of the population of the city has a private health insurance. This can includes hospitals, private practices and pharmacies. The city of São Paulo has the largest number of foreigners comparing with any other Brazilian city and an intense [health tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_tourism). In Brazil, the city of São Paulo has the largest number of doctors who can speak more than one language, which in this case is [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language), with the secondary languages predominantly are [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) and [Spanish](26825.md).[^cite_note-186]

The private health care sector is very large and most of Brazil's best hospitals are in the city. As of September 2009, the city of São Paulo had: 32,553 [ambulatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambulatory) clinics, centers and professional offices (physicians, dentists and others); 217 hospitals, with 32,554 beds; 137,745 health care professionals, including 28,316 physicians.[^cite_note-187]

The municipal government operates public [health facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_facilities) across the city's territory, with 770 primary health care units (UBS), ambulatory and emergency clinics and 17 hospitals. The Municipal Secretary of Health has 59,000 employees, including 8,000 physicians and 12,000 nurses. 6,000,000 citizens uses the facilities, which provide drugs at no cost and manage an extensive family health program (PSF – Programa de Saúde da Família).[^cite_note-SIGA_Saúde-188]

The Sistema Integrado de Gestão de Assistência à Saúde de São Paulo – SIGA Saúde (*Integrated Health Care Management System in São Paulo*) has been operating in the city of São Paulo since 2004. Today there are more than 22 million registered users, including the people of the [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), with a monthly average of 1.3 million appointments.[^cite_note-SIGA_Saúde-188]

## Government

See also: [List of mayors of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[Matarazzo Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matarazzo_Building), the [São Paulo city hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seat_of_local_government)



[Municipal Chamber of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Chamber_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[Bandeirantes Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_dos_Bandeirantes), the seat of state government



[Legislative Assembly of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

As the capital of the [state of São Paulo](229422.md), the city is home to the [Bandeirantes Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_dos_Bandeirantes) (state government) and the [Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). The Executive Branch of the municipality of São Paulo is represented by the [mayor](101146.md) and his cabinet of secretaries, following the model proposed by the [Federal Constitution](26847507.md).[^cite_note-189] The organic law of the municipality and the Master Plan of the city, however, determine that the public administration must guarantee to the population effective tools of manifestation of participatory democracy, which causes that the city is divided in regional prefectures, each one led by a Regional Mayor appointed by the Mayor.[^cite_note-districts-190]

The legislative power is represented by the Municipal Chamber, composed of 55 aldermen elected to four-year posts (in compliance with the provisions of Article 29 of the Constitution, which dictates a minimum number of 42 and a maximum of 55 for municipalities with more than five million inhabitants). It is up to the house to draft and vote fundamental laws for the administration and the Executive, especially the municipal budget (known as the Law of Budgetary Guidelines).[^cite_note-191] In addition to the legislative process and the work of the secretariats, there are also a number of municipal councils, each dealing with different topics, composed of representatives of the various sectors of organized civil society. The actual performance and representativeness of such councils, however, are sometimes questioned.

### Subdivisions



Location on a map of [municipalities of the state of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

São Paulo is divided into 32 [subprefectures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subprefecture), each with an administration ("subprefeitura") divided into several districts ("distritos").[^cite_note-districts-190] The city also has a radial division into nine zones for purpose of traffic control and bus lines, which do not fit into the administrative divisions. These zones are identified by colors in the street signs. The historical core of São Paulo, which includes the inner city and the area of [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), is in the [Subprefecture of Sé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subprefecture_of_S%C3%A9). Most other economic and tourist facilities of the city are inside an area officially called *Centro Expandido* (Portuguese for "Broad Center", or "Broad Downtown"), which includes Sé and several other subprefectures, and areas immediately around it.
