## Economy

Main articles: [Economy of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Economy of the state of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_state_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), and [Economy of Brazil](3634.md)



[Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), the headquarters of many financial and cultural institutions



[Brigadeiro Faria Lima Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadeiro_Faria_Lima_Avenue), the main [financial district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_financial_districts) of São Paulo

São Paulo is the city with the largest share of Brazil's GDP and has one of the [highest city GDPs in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_GDP).[^cite_note-Estadão-194][^cite_note-195] According to data from the [IBGE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Institute_of_Geography_and_Statistics), its gross domestic product (GDP) in 2010 was R\$450 billion,[^cite_note-196] approximately US\$220 billion, 12.26% of Brazilian GDP and 36% of the São Paulo state's GDP.[^cite_note-197] The per capita income for the city was R\$47.802,02 in 2022.[^cite_note-198]

São Paulo is considered the [financial capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_capital) of Brazil, as it is the location for the headquarters of major corporations and of banks and financial institutions. The city is the headquarters of [B3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B3_\(stock_exchange\)), the [largest stock exchange of Latin America by market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_major_stock_exchanges),[^cite_note-B3-21] and has several [financial districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_financial_districts), mainly in the areas around [Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), [Faria Lima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadeiro_Faria_Lima_Avenue) and [Berrini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engenheiro_Lu%C3%ADs_Carlos_Berrini_Avenue) avenues. 63% of all the [international companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company) with business in Brazil have their head offices in São Paulo. São Paulo has one of the largest concentrations of German businesses worldwide[^cite_note-199] and is the largest Swedish industrial hub alongside [Gothenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothenburg).[^cite_note-200]



[B3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B3_\(stock_exchange\)), the [largest stock exchange of Latin America by market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_major_stock_exchanges)

[^cite_note-201]



[United Nations Business Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centro_Empresarial_Na%C3%A7%C3%B5es_Unidas) at [Brooklin Novo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklin_Novo)



[Oscar Freire St.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rua_Oscar_Freire), one of the most luxurious streets in Brazil

[^cite_note-202]

As of 2014, São Paulo is the third largest exporting municipality in Brazil after [Parauapebas, PA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parauapebas) and [Rio de Janeiro, RJ](25936.md). In that year São Paulo's exported goods totaled US\$7.32B or 3.02% of Brazil's total exports. The top five [commodities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commodity_\(type_of_good\)) exported by São Paulo are [soybean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soybean) (21%), [raw sugar](27712.md) (19%), [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee) (6.5%), [sulfate chemical wood pulp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulp_\(paper\)) (5.6%), and [corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize) (4.4%).[^cite_note-203]

São Paulo's economy is going through a deep transformation. Once a city with a strong [industrial character](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sector_of_the_economy), São Paulo's economy has followed the global trend of shifting to the [tertiary sector of the economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy), focusing on services. São Paulo also has a large "informal" economy.[^cite_note-204] According to [PricewaterhouseCoopers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PricewaterhouseCoopers) average annual economic growth of the city is 4.2%.[^cite_note-205] In 2005, the city of São Paulo collected R\$90 billion in taxes and the city budget was R\$15 billion. The city has 1,500 bank branches and 70 shopping malls.[^cite_note-206]

The city is unique among Brazilian cities for its large number of foreign corporations.[^cite_note-207] São Paulo ranked second after New York in [FDi magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FDi_magazine)'s bi-annual ranking of Cities of the Future 2013–14 in the [Americas](29833.md), and was named the Latin American City of the Future 2013–14.[^cite_note-208] According to [Mercer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercer_\(consulting_firm\))'s 2011 [city rankings of cost of living for expatriate employees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_cities_for_expatriate_employees#Mercer_surveys), São Paulo is among the ten most expensive cities in the world.[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210]

[Luxury brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxury_brand) tend to concentrate their business in São Paulo. Because of the lack of department stores and multi-brand boutiques, [shopping malls](235723.md) as well as the [Jardins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jardins) district attract most of the world's luxurious brands. Most of the international luxury brands can be found in the [Iguatemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguatemi_Empresa_de_Shopping_Centers), Cidade Jardim or JK shopping malls or on the streets of [Oscar Freire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rua_Oscar_Freire), Lorena or Haddock Lobo in the Jardins district. They are home of brands such as [Cartier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartier_\(jeweler\)), [Chanel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chanel), [Dior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dior), [Giorgio Armani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Armani), [Gucci](844136.md), [Lis Vuitton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Vuitton), [Marc Jacobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Jacobs), [Tiffany & Co](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiffany_&_Co). Cidade Jardim was opened in São Paulo in 2008, it is a 45,000-square-meter (484,376-square-foot) mall, landscaped with trees and greenery scenario, with a focus on Brazilian brands but also home to international luxury brands such as [Hermès](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herm%C3%A8s), [Jimmy Choo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Choo), [Pucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_Pucci) and [Carolina Herrera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina_Herrera_\(fashion_designer\)). Opened in 2012, JK shopping mall has brought to Brazil brands that were not present in the country before such as Goyard, Tory Burch, Llc., [Prada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prada), and Miu Miu.[^cite_note-211]

The [Iguatemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iguatemi_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) Faria Lima, in [Faria Lima Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenida_Brigadeiro_Faria_Lima), is Brazil's oldest mall, opened in 1966.[^cite_note-212] The [Jardins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jardins) region, which includes the neighbourhoods all comprised within the [Subprefecture of Pinheiros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subprefecture_of_Pinheiros).[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in the city of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_the_city_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[Ibirapuera Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibirapuera_Park), elected the best in South America by [TripAdvisor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TripAdvisor)

[^cite_note-215]



[Roda Rico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roda_Rico), the tallest [observation wheel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observation_wheel) in Latin America

[^cite_note-Estrutura-216]



[Anhangabaú Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anhangaba%C3%BA_Valley) in the [Historic Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Center_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[People's Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Park_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) in [Itaim Bibi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itaim_Bibi)

Large hotel chains whose target audience is the corporate traveler are in the city. São Paulo is home to 75% of the country's leading business fairs. The city also promotes one of the most important fashion weeks in the world, [São Paulo Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Fashion_Week), established in 1996 under the name Morumbi Fashion Brasil, is the largest and most important fashion event in Latin America.[^cite_note-217] Besides, the [São Paulo Gay Pride Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Gay_Pride_Parade), held since 1997 on [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue) is the event that attracts more tourists to the city.[^cite_note-218]

The annual March For Jesus is a large gathering of Christians from Protestant churches throughout Brazil, with São Paulo police reporting participation in the range of 350,000 in 2015.[^cite_note-219] In addition, São Paulo hosts the annual São Paulo Pancake Cook-Off in which chefs from across Brazil and the world participate in competitions based on the cooking of [pancakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pancakes).[^cite_note-220]

São Paulo has been named the 18th best city in the world by the 2026 World's Best Cities Report, standing out among 270 global destinations evaluated by Resonance. The city leads all of Latin America, ahead of Mexico City, Buenos Aires, Rio de Janeiro, Bogotá and Lima.[^cite_note-221]

The city has nightlife considered one of the best in the country, and is an international hub of active and diverse [nightlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nightlife) with [bars](272207.md), dance bars and [nightclubs](19321330.md) staying open well past midnight.[^cite_note-222] The area offers cinemas, theaters, museums, and cultural centers. The [Rua Oscar Freire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rua_Oscar_Freire) was named one of the "eight most luxurious streets in the world", according to the Mystery Shopping International,[^cite_note-Oscar_Freire-223] and São Paulo the 25th "most expensive city" of the planet.[^cite_note-224]

According to the International Congress & Convention Association, São Paulo ranks first among the cities that host international events in [Americas](29833.md) and the 12th in the world, after [Vienna](55866.md), [Paris](22989.md), [Barcelona](4443.md), [Singapore](27318.md), [Berlin](3354.md), [Budapest](36787.md), [Amsterdam](844.md), [Stockholm](26741.md), [Seoul](19159283.md), [Lisbon](18091.md), and [Copenhagen](5166.md).[^cite_note-225]

According to a study by [MasterCard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MasterCard) in 130 cities around the world, São Paulo was the third most visited destination in Latin America (behind [Mexico City](18987.md) and [Buenos Aires](255919.md)) with 2.4 million foreign travelers, who spent US\$2.9 billion in 2013 (the highest among the cities in the region). In 2014, [CNN](62028.md) ranked nightlife São Paulo as the fourth best in the world, behind [New York City](645042.md), [Berlin](3354.md) and [Ibiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibiza), in [Spain](26667.md).[^cite_note-226]

The cuisine of the region is another tourist attraction. The city has 62 cuisines across 12,000 restaurants.[^cite_note-227] During the 10th International Congress of Gastronomy, Hospitality and Tourism (Cihat) conducted in 1997, the city received the title of "World Gastronomy Capital" from a commission formed by 43 nations' representatives.[^cite_note-228]

## Infrastructure



The city view from [Altino Arantes Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altino_Arantes_Building)



Changes in urban fabrics in the region of [Jardins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jardins): side by side, vertical areas and low houses

Since the beginning of the 20th century, São Paulo has been one of the main economic centers of Latin America. With the [First](4764461.md) and [Second World Wars](32927.md) and the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression), coffee exports to the [United States](3434750.md) and [Europe](9239.md) were heavily affected, forcing the rich coffee growers to invest in the industrial activities that would make São Paulo the largest industrial center in Brazil. The new job vacancies contributed to attract a significant number of immigrants (mainly from Italy)[^cite_note-Italian-229] and migrants, especially from the Northeastern states.[^cite_note-Causas-230] From a population of only 32.000 people in 1880, São Paulo now had 8.5 million inhabitants in 1980. The rapid population growth has brought many problems for the city.

According to data from IBGE and Eletropaulo, the electricity grid serves almost 100% of households. The fixed telephony network is still precarious, with coverage of 67.2%. Household garbage collection covers all regions of the municipality but is still insufficient, reaching around 94% of the demand in districts such as [Parelheiros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parelheiros_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) and Perus. About 80% of the garbage produced daily by Paulistas is exported to other cities, such as [Caieiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caieiras) and [Guarulhos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guarulhos).[^cite_note-lixo-231] Recycling accounts for about 1% of the 15,000 metric tons of waste produced daily.[^cite_note-lixo-231]

### Urban planning

| icon | This section **may need to be rewritten to comply with Wikipedia's [quality standards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Manual_of_Style)**, as Vague, abstract language ("socio-economic centralities"); probably way too much detail about the development of the city (with no indication of what period of time is being discussed) for an overview article about the city.. Relevant discussion may be found on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:S%C3%A3o_Paulo#). [You can help](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Paulo\&action=edit). The talk page may contain suggestions. *(July 2025)* |
| --- | --- |



[Marginal Tietê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marginal_Tiet%C3%AA), the main [expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled-access_highway) of the city



[Marginal Pinheiros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marginal_Pinheiros)

The original nuclei of the city are vertical, characterized by the presence of commercial buildings and services. The peripheries are generally developed with two to four-story buildings, though there are exceptions. Compared to other global cities (such as the island cities of [New York City](645042.md) and [Hong Kong](13404.md)), however, São Paulo is considered a "low-rise building" city. Its tallest buildings rarely reach forty stories, and the average residential building has twenty. Nevertheless, it is the fourth city in the world in quantity of buildings,[^cite_note-232] besides possessing what was considered until 2014 the tallest skyscraper of the country, the [Mirante do Vale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirante_do_Vale), also known as *Palácio Zarzur Kogan*, with 170 meters. Currently the tallest building in the city is [Alto Das Nações](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alto_das_Na%C3%A7%C3%B5es) at 219 meters [^cite_note-233]

Some central regions of the city began to concentrate indigents, [drug trafficking](218449.md), street vending and [prostitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prostitution), which encouraged the creation of new socio-economic centralities. The characterization of each region of the city also underwent several changes throughout the 20th century. With the relocation of industries to other cities or states, several areas that once housed factory sheds have become commercial or even residential areas.[^cite_note-Planejamento-234]

São Paulo has a history of actions, projects and plans related to urban planning that can be traced to the governments of Antonio da Silva Prado, Baron Duprat, Washington and Luis Francisco Prestes Maia. However, in general, the city was formed during the 20th century, growing from village to metropolis through a series of informal processes and irregular urban sprawl.[^cite_note-235]

Urban growth in São Paulo has followed three patterns since the beginning of the 20th century, according to urban historians: since the late 19th century and until the 1940s, São Paulo was a condensed city in which different social groups lived in a small urban zone separated by type of housing; from the 1940s to the 1980s, São Paulo followed a model of center-periphery social segregation, in which the upper and middle-classes occupied central and modern areas while the poor moved towards precarious, self-built housing in the periphery; and from the 1980s onward, new transformations have brought the social classes closer together in spatial terms, but separated by walls and security technologies that seek to isolate the richer classes in the name of security.[^cite_note-Caldeira,_Teresa_P.R._2000._p._215-236] Thus, São Paulo differs considerably from other Brazilian cities such as [Belo Horizonte](82198.md) and [Goiânia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goi%C3%A2nia), whose initial expansion followed determinations by a plan, or a city like [Brasília](4752.md), whose master plan had been fully developed prior to construction.[^cite_note-237]

The effectiveness of these plans has been seen by some planners and historians as questionable. Some of these scholars argue that such plans were produced exclusively for the benefit of the wealthier strata of the population while the working classes would be relegated to the traditional informal processes. In São Paulo until the mid-1950s, the plans were based on the idea of "demolish and rebuild", including former Mayor [Francisco Prestes Maia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Prestes_Maia)'s road plan for São Paulo (known as the Avenues Plan) or [Saturnino de Brito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturnino_de_Brito)'s plan for the Tietê River. The Plan of the Avenues was implemented during the 1920s and sought to build large avenues connecting the city center with the outskirts. This plan included renewing the commercial city center, leading to real estate speculation and gentrification of several city centre districts. The plan also led to the expansion of bus services, which would soon replace the trolley as the preliminary transportation system.[^cite_note-238] This contributed to the outwards expansion of São Paulo and the peripherization of poorer residents. Peripheral districts were usually unregulated and consisted mainly of self-built single-family houses.[^cite_note-Caldeira,_Teresa_P.R._2000._p._215-236]

In 1968 the Urban Development Plan proposed the Basic Plan for Integrated Development of São Paulo, under the administration of Figueiredo Ferraz. The main result was zoning laws. It lasted until 2004 when the Basic Plan was replaced by the current Master Plan.[^cite_note-239] That [zoning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoning_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), adopted in 1972, designated "Z1" areas ([residential areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential_area) designed for elites) and "Z3" (a "mixed zone" lacking clear definitions about their characteristics). Zoning encouraged the growth of suburbs with minimal control and major speculation.[^cite_note-240] After the 1970s peripheral lot regulation increased and infrastructure in the periphery improved, driving land prices up. The poorest and the newcomers now could not purchase their lot and build their house, and were forced to look for a housing alternative. As a result, [favelas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Favela) and precarious tenements (cortiços) appeared.[^cite_note-241] These housing types were often closer to the city's center: favelas could sprawl in any unused terrain (often dangerous or unsanitary) and decaying or abandoned buildings for tenements were abundant inside the city. Favelas went back into the urban perimeter, occupying the small lots not yet occupied by urbanization – alongside polluted rivers, railways, or between bridges.[^cite_note-242] By 1993, 19.8% of São Paulo's population lived in [favelas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Favelas), compared to 5.2% in 1980.[^cite_note-243] Today, it is estimated that 2.1 million Paulistas live in favelas, which represents about 11% of the metropolitan area's population.[^cite_note-244]



Panoramic view of [Central Zone of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Zone_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) from [Italy Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edif%C3%ADcio_It%C3%A1lia)

### Transport

Main articles: [Transport in São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) and [Transport in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Brazil)

#### Airports



[São Paulo–Congonhas Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo%E2%80%93Congonhas_Airport)



[São Paulo/Guarulhos International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo/Guarulhos_International_Airport)

São Paulo has two main airports, [São Paulo/Guarulhos International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo/Guarulhos_International_Airport) for international flights and national hub, and [São Paulo–Congonhas Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo%E2%80%93Congonhas_Airport) for domestic and regional flights. Another airport, the [Campo de Marte Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campo_de_Marte_Airport), serves private jets and light aircraft. The three airports together moved more than 58,000,000 passengers in 2015, making São Paulo one of the top 15 busiest in the world, by number of air passenger movements. The region of Greater São Paulo is also served by [Viracopos International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viracopos_International_Airport), [São José dos Campos Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Jos%C3%A9_dos_Campos_Airport) and [Jundiaí Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jundia%C3%AD_Airport).

Congonhas Airport operates flights mainly to Rio de Janeiro, Porto Alegre, Belo Horizonte and Brasília. Built in the 1930s, it was designed to handle the increasing demand for flights, in the fastest growing city in the world. Located in Campo Belo District, Congonhas Airport is close to the three main city's financial districts: [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue), [Brigadeiro Faria Lima Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadeiro_Faria_Lima_Avenue) and [Engenheiro Luís Carlos Berrini Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engenheiro_Lu%C3%ADs_Carlos_Berrini_Avenue).

The São Paulo–Guarulhos International, also known as "Cumbica", is 25 km (16 mi) north-east of the city center, in the [Guarulhos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guarulhos) municipality. Every day nearly 110.000 people pass through the airport, which connects Brazil to 36 countries around the world. 370 companies operate there, generating more than 53.000 jobs. The international airport is connected to the metropolitan rail system, with [Line 13 (CPTM)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_13_\(CPTM\)).

Campo de Marte is in [Santana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santana_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) district, the northern [zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoning) of São Paulo. The airport handles private flights and air shuttles, including [air taxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_taxi) firms. Opened in 1935, Campo de Marte is the base for the largest helicopter fleet in Brazil and the world's, ahead of New York and Tokyo.[^cite_note-245] This airport is the home base of the State Civil Police Air Tactical Unit, the State Military Police Radio Patrol Unit and the São Paulo Flying Club.[^cite_note-246] From this airport, passengers can take advantage of some 350 remote helipads and heliports to bypass heavy road traffic.[^cite_note-247]

#### Roads



[Bandeirantes Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_dos_Bandeirantes)/SP-348



[Mario Covas Beltway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodoanel_M%C3%A1rio_Covas)

Automobiles are the main means to get into the city. In March 2011, more than 7 million vehicles were registered.[^cite_note-248] Heavy traffic is common on the city's main avenues and [traffic jams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_congestion) are relatively common on its highways.

The city is crossed by 10 major motorways: [President Dutra Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Presidente_Dutra)/BR-116 (connects São Paulo to the east and [north-east](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinal_direction) of the country); Régis Bittencourt Highway/BR-116 (connects São Paulo to the south of the country); [Fernão Dias Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Fern%C3%A3o_Dias)/BR-381 (connects São Paulo to the north of the country); [Anchieta Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Anchieta)/SP-150 (connects São Paulo to the ocean coast); [Immigrants Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_dos_Imigrantes)/SP-150 (connects São Paulo to the ocean coast); [President Castelo Branco Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Castelo_Branco)/SP-280 (connects São Paulo to the west and north-west of the country); [Raposo Tavares Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Raposo_Tavares)/SP-270 (connects São Paulo to the west of the country); [Anhanguera Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Anhanguera)/SP-330 (connects São Paulo to the north-west of the country, including its capital city); [Bandeirantes Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_dos_Bandeirantes)/SP-348 (connects São Paulo to the north-west of the country); [Ayrton Senna Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodovia_Ayrton_Senna)/SP-70 (named after Brazilian [Formula One](10854.md) driver [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna), the motorway connects São Paulo to east locations of the state, as well as the north coast of the state).

The [Rodoanel Mário Covas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodoanel_M%C3%A1rio_Covas) (official designation SP-021) is the [beltway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beltway) of the [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). Upon its completion, it will have a length of 177 km (110 mi), with a radius of approximately 23 km (14 mi) from the geographical center of the city. It was named after Mário Covas, who was mayor of the city of São Paulo (1983–1985) and a state governor (1994–1998/1998–2001) until his death from cancer. It is a controlled access highway with a speed limit of 100 km/h (62 mph) under normal weather and traffic circumstances. The west, south and east parts are completed, and the north part, which will close the beltway, is due in 2026 and is being built by DERSA.[^cite_note-249]

#### Buses



São Paulo has [two independent trolleybus systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybuses_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo).

[^cite_note-janes2011-250]



[Tietê Bus Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_Bus_Terminal), the 2nd busiest [bus terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_station) in the world, after [PABT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Bus_Terminal)

[^cite_note-Tietê_Bus_Terminal-251]

Main articles: [SPTrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SPTrans), [Empresa Metropolitana de Transportes Urbanos de São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empresa_Metropolitana_de_Transportes_Urbanos_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), and [Trolleybuses in São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybuses_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

Bus transport (government and private) is composed of 17,000 buses (including about 290 [trolley buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybus)).[^cite_note-juts2009-252] The traditional system of informal transport (dab vans) was later reorganized and legalized. The [trolleybus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybus) systems provide a portion of the [public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport) service in [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) with two independent networks.[^cite_note-moraes1999-253][^cite_note-janes2011-250] The [SPTrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SPTrans) (São Paulo Transportes) system opened in 1949 and serves the city of São Paulo, while the [Empresa Metropolitana de Transportes Urbanos de São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empresa_Metropolitana_de_Transportes_Urbanos_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) (EMTU) system opened in 1988 and serves suburban areas to the southeast of the city proper. Worldwide, São Paulo is one of only two metropolitan areas possessing two independent trolleybus systems, the other being [Naples, Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybuses_in_Naples).[^cite_note-janes2011-250]

São Paulo [Tietê Bus Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiet%C3%AA_Bus_Terminal) is the second largest [bus terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_station) in the world, after [PABT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Bus_Terminal) in [New York](645042.md).[^cite_note-Tietê_Bus_Terminal-251] It serves localities across the nation, with the exception of the states of [Amazonas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazonas_\(Brazilian_state\)), Roraima and [Amapá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amap%C3%A1). Routes to 1,010 cities in five countries (Brazil, Argentina, Chile, Uruguay and Paraguay) are available. It connects to all regional airports and a [ride sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpool) automobile service to [Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santos,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo).[^cite_note-Tietê_Bus_Terminal-251]

The [Palmeiras-Barra Funda Intermodal Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmeiras-Barra_Funda_Intermodal_Terminal) is much smaller and is connected to the Palmeiras-Barra Funda metro and Palmeiras-Barra Funda CPTM stations. It serves the southwestern cities of [Sorocaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorocaba), Itapetininga, Itu, Botucatu, [Bauru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauru), [Marília](901279.md), [Jaú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ja%C3%BA), [Avaré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avar%C3%A9,_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Piraju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piraju), [Santa Cruz do Rio Pardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Cruz_do_Rio_Pardo), [Ipaussu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipaussu), [Chavantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chavantes) and [Ourinhos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ourinhos) (on the border with [Paraná](229411.md) State). It also serves [São José do Rio Preto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Jos%C3%A9_do_Rio_Preto), [Araçatuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ara%C3%A7atuba) and other small towns on the northwest of São Paulo State.

#### Urban rail



Map of the 377 km (234 mi) [urban rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_rail) network of the city ([São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro) and [CPTM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Companhia_Paulista_de_Trens_Metropolitanos))



[Monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monorail) of the [Line 15 (Silver)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_15_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro\)) of the [São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro)



Train of the [Line 4 (Yellow)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_4_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro\)) of the [São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro)



Train of the [CPTM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Companhia_Paulista_de_Trens_Metropolitanos) at [Luz Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luz_Station)

Main articles: [São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro) and [São Paulo Metropolitan Trains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metropolitan_Trains)

São Paulo has an [urban rail transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_rail_transit) system ([São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro) and [São Paulo Metropolitan Trains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metropolitan_Trains)) that serves 184 stations and has 377 km (234 mi) of track,[^cite_note-254] forming the largest [metropolitan rail transport network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo#Rail_transport) of [Latin America](18524.md).[^cite_note-255] The underground and urban railway lines together carry some 7 million people on an average weekday.[^cite_note-256]

The [São Paulo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro) operates 104 km (65 mi) of [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) system, with six lines in operation, serving 91 stations.[^cite_note-spmetro_about-257] In 2015, the metro reached the mark of 11.5 million passengers per mile of line, 15% higher than in 2008, when 10 million users were taken per mile. It is the largest concentration of people in a single transport system in the world, according to the company. In 2014, the São Paulo Metro was elected the best metro system in the [Americas](29833.md).

The company ViaQuatro, a [private concessionaire](3261664.md), operates the [Line 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_4_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro\)) of the metro system.[^cite_note-258] The [Line 15 (Silver)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_15_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro\)) is the first mass-transit [monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monorail) of the South America and the first system in the world to use the [Bombardier Innovia Monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Innovia_Monorail) 300. When fully completed will be the largest and highest capacity monorail system in the Americas and second worldwide, only behind to the [Chongqing Monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chongqing_Rail_Transit).[^cite_note-259]

The [Companhia Paulista de Trens Metropolitanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Companhia_Paulista_de_Trens_Metropolitanos) (CPTM, or "Paulista Company of Metropolitan Trains") railway add 273.0 km (169.6 mi) of [commuter rail](6286.md), with seven lines and 94 stations. The system carries about 2.8 million passengers a day. On 8 June 2018, CPTM set a weekday ridership record with 3,096,035 trips.[^cite_note-260] The [Line 13 (Jade)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_13_\(CPTM\)) of the CPTM connects São Paulo to the [São Paulo–Guarulhos International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo%E2%80%93Guarulhos_International_Airport), in the municipality of [Guarulhos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guarulhos), the first major international airport in South America to be directly served by train.[^cite_note-261]

[CCR Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCR_S.A.) (through the [ViaQuatro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ViaQuatro) and [ViaMobilidade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ViaMobilidade) concessionaires) operates subway lines [4–Yellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_4_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro\)) and [5–Lilac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_5_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Metro\)), in addition to managing (through the ViaMobilidade concessionaire) lines [8-Diamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_8_\(CPTM\)) and [9-Emerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_9_\(CPTM\)) of the metropolitan train system. Metro and metropolitan train networks transport an average of nearly 7 million people a day, while another 2 million passengers are transported by EMTU buses daily.[^cite_note-262]

The two major São Paulo [railway stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_stations) are Luz and Júlio Prestes in the Luz/[Campos Eliseos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campos_El%C3%ADseos_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) region. [Julio Prestes Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_J%C3%BAlio_Prestes) connected Southwest São Paulo State and Northern [Paraná](229411.md) State to São Paulo City. Agricultural products were transferred to Luz Station from which they headed to the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md) and overseas. Júlio Prestes stopped transporting passengers through the Sorocabana or FEPASA lines and now only has metro service. Due to its acoustics and interior beauty, surrounded by Greek revival columns, part of the rebuilt station was transformed into the São Paulo Hall.

[Luz Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luz_Station) was built in Britain and assembled in Brazil. It has an underground station and is still active with metro lines that link São Paulo to the Greater São Paulo region to the East and the Campinas Metropolitan region in [Jundiaí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jundia%C3%AD) in the western part of the State. Luz Station is surrounded by important [cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture) institutions such as the [Pinacoteca do Estado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinacoteca_do_Estado), The Museu de Arte Sacra on Tiradentes Avenue and Jardim da Luz, among others. It is the seat of the [Santos-Jundiaí line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estrada_de_Ferro_Santos-Jundia%C3%AD) which historically transported [international immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Brazil) from the [Port of Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Santos) to São Paulo and the coffee plantation lands in the Western region of [Campinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campinas). São Paulo has no [tram](30733.md) lines, although trams were common in the first half of the 20th century.[^cite_note-263]

## Culture

### Music

Main article: [Music of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Brazil)



[Sala São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sala_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), the home of the [São Paulo State Symphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_State_Symphony)

São Paulo and Rio de Janeiro are considered the centre of the [urban music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_contemporary) movement in Brazil.[^cite_note-264]

[Adoniran Barbosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoniran_Barbosa) was a [samba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samba) singer and composer who became successful during São Paulo's early radio era. Born in 1912 in the town of [Valinhos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valinhos), Barbosa was known as the "composer to the masses", particularly Italian immigrants living in the quarters of Bela Vista, also known as "Bexiga" and Brás, as well as those who lived in the city's many 'cortiços' or tenements. His songs drew from the life of urban workers, the unemployed and those who lived on the edge. His first big hit was "Saudosa Maloca" ("Shanty of Fond Memories" – 1951), wherein three homeless friends recall with nostalgia their improvised shanty home, which was torn down by the landowner to make room for a building. His 1964 [Trem das Onze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trem_das_Onze) ("The 11 pm Train"), became one of the five best samba songs ever, the protagonist explains to his lover that he cannot stay any longer because he has to catch the last train to the [Jaçanã suburb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ja%C3%A7an%C3%A3_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), for his mother will not sleep before he arrives home. Another important musician with a similar style is [Paulo Vanzolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Vanzolini). Vanzolini is a PhD in biology and a part-time professional musician. He composed a song depicting a love murder scene in São Paulo called "Ronda".



[Ibirapuera Auditorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibirapuera_Auditorium)



[Titãs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tit%C3%A3s) live at the Montevideo Rock 2018

In the late 1960s, a psychedelic rock band called [Os Mutantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Os_Mutantes) became popular. Their success is related to that of other [tropicalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropicalismo) musicians. The group was known as very *paulistanos* in its behavior and clothing. Os Mutantes released five albums before lead singer [Rita Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Lee) departed in 1972 to join another group called [Tutti Frutti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutti_Frutti_\(Brazilian_band\)). Although initially known only in Brazil, Os Mutantes became successful abroad after the 1990s. In 2000, *[Tecnicolor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tecnicolor)*, an album recorded in the early 1970s in English by the band, was released with artwork designed by [Sean Lennon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Lennon).[^cite_note-265]

In the early 1980s, a band called *[RPM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RPM_\(band\))* was very successful in the 1980s, being considered [The Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles) of Brazil. Their live album *Rádio Pirata ao vivo* was the best-selling Brazilian record ever, with more than 3 million copies sold.[^cite_note-266] The group's success was not limited to CD sales. RPM started a phenomenon that was compared to [Beatlemania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatlemania), with enthusiastic fans filling stadiums, stopping traffic, evading security, and buying any products with the RPM brand.

A late punk and garage scene became strong in the 1980s in São Paulo, perhaps associated with the gloomy scenario of unemployment during an extended recession. Bands originating from this movement include [Ultraje a Rigor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultraje_a_Rigor), [Ira!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ira!), [Titãs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tit%C3%A3s), [Ratos de Porão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratos_de_Por%C3%A3o) and [Inocentes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inocentes). In the 1990s, [drum and bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_and_bass) arose as another musical movement in São Paulo, with artists such as [DJ Marky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_Marky), [DJ Patife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_Patife), [XRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xerxes_de_Oliveira), [Drumagick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drumagick) and [Fernanda Porto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernanda_Porto).[^cite_note-267] Many [heavy metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_metal_music) bands also originated in São Paulo, such as [Angra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angra_\(band\)), [Project46](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project46), [Torture Squad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torture_Squad), Korzus and [Dr. Sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Sin). Famous electro-pop band [Cansei de Ser Sexy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSS_\(band\)), or CSS (Portuguese for "tired of being sexy") also has its origins in the city.

Many of the most important classical Brazilian living composers, such as [Amaral Vieira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Carlos_Amaral_Vieira), [Osvaldo Lacerda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osvaldo_Lacerda) and [Edson Zampronha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edson_Zampronha), were born and live in São Paulo. Local [baritone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baritone) [Paulo Szot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Szot) has won international acclaim performing for six consecutive seasons at The Metropolitan Opera, La Scala and Opera de Paris, among others; and The [Tony Award](54741.md) for best actor in a musical for his performance in a 2008 revival of *[South Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Pacific_\(musical\))*. The [São Paulo State Symphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orquestra_Sinf%C3%B4nica_do_Estado_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) is one of the world's outstanding orchestras; their artistic director beginning in 2012 is the noted American conductor [Marin Alsop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_Alsop). In 1952, [Heitor Villa-Lobos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heitor_Villa-Lobos) wrote his Symphony Number 10 ('Ameríndia') for the 400th anniversary of São Paulo: an allegorical, historical and religious account of the city told through the eyes of its founder [José de Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Anchieta).[^cite_note-268]

São Paulo's opera houses are: [São Paulo Municipal Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatro_Municipal_\(S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)), Theatro São Pedro and Alfa Theater, for the symphonic concerts there is the [Sala São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sala_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), the latter being the headquarters of [OSESP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSESP), an orchestra. The city hosts several music halls. The main ones are: Citibank Hall, HSBC Music Hall, Olympia, Via Funchal, Villa Country, Arena Anhembi and Espaco das Américas. The [Anhembi Sambadrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anhembi_Sambadrome) hosts musical presentations as well, in addition to the [Carnival of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnival_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo). Other facilities include the new [Praça das Artes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pra%C3%A7a_das_Artes), with the Municipal Conservatory of Music Chamber Hall and others venues, like, Cultura Artistica, Teatro Sérgio Cardoso with a venue for only dance performances and Herzog & DeMeron's Centro Cultural Luz, for Ballet, Opera, theater and concerts, with three huge halls. The auditorium of the Latin-American Cultural Center, The Mozarteum, holds concerts through the year. Festivals as the [Virada Cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virada_Cultural) (Cultural Overnight) happen once a year and holds hundreds of attractions spread throughout the city.



2007 [Virada Cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virada_Cultural), in [Downtown São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

### Literature

Main article: [Literature of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literature_of_Brazil)



[Mário de Andrade Library](23196976.md)



[Livraria Cultura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livraria_Cultura)

São Paulo was home to the first [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus) missionaries in Brazil, in the early 16th century. They wrote reports to the Portuguese crown about the newly found land, the [native peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples) and composed poetry and music for the [catechism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism), creating the first written works from the area. The literary priests included [Manuel da Nóbrega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_da_N%C3%B3brega) and [José de Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Anchieta), living in or near the colony then called *Piratininga*. They also helped to register the [Old Tupi language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Tupi_language), lexicon and its grammar. In 1922, the Brazilian Modernist Movement, launched in São Paulo, began to achieve cultural independence. Brazil had gone through the same stages of development as the rest of Latin America, but its political and cultural [independence](62450.md) came more gradually.[^cite_note-269]

Brazilian elite culture was originally strongly tied to [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Portugal). Gradually writers developed a multi-ethnic body of work that was distinctively Brazilian. The presence of large numbers of former slaves added a distinctive [African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_of_Africa) character to the [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Brazil). Subsequent infusions of immigrants of non-Portuguese origin broadened the range of influences.[^cite_note-Modernism-270]

[Mário de Andrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1rio_de_Andrade) and [Oswald de Andrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oswald_de_Andrade) were the prototypical modernists. With the urban poems of "Paulicéia Desvairada" and "Carefree Paulistan land" (1922), Mário de Andrade established the [movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_movement) in Brazil. His rhapsodic novel *Macunaíma* (1928), with its abundance of Brazilian [folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore), represents the apex of [modernism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernism)'s nationalist prose through its creation of an offbeat native [national hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hero). Oswald de Andrade's experimental poetry, [avant-garde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avant-garde) prose, particularly the novel Serafim Ponte Grande (1933) and provocative manifestos exemplify the movement's break with tradition.[^cite_note-Modernism-270]

Modernist artists and writers chose the Municipal Theatre of São Paulo to launch their Modernist manifesto. The site happened to be a bastion of European culture with opera and classical music presentations from Germany, France, Austria and Italy. They defied the high society that frequented the venue and who insisted on speaking only foreign languages such as French, behaving as if Brazilian culture did not matter.[^cite_note-Modernism-270]

### Theaters



[Municipal Theatre of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Theatre_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

Many historians believe that the first theatrical performance in Brazil was held in São Paulo. The Portuguese [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus) missionary [José de Anchieta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Anchieta) (1534–1597) wrote short plays that were performed and watched by the Tupi–Guarani natives. In the second half of the 19th century, a cultural, musical and theatrical life emerged. [European ethnic groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Europe) began holding performances in some of the state's rural cities. The most important period for the art in São Paulo was the 1940s. São Paulo had had a professional company, Teatro Brasileiro de Comédia, (Brazilian Theater of Comedy), along with others.

During the 1960s, major theater productions in São Paulo and Brazil were presented by two groups. Teatro de Arena began with a group of students from Escola de Arte Dramática (Drama [Art School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_school)), founded by Alfredo Mesquita, in 1948. In 1958, the group excelled with the play "Eles não usam black tie" by [Gianfrancesco Guarnieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianfrancesco_Guarnieri) which was the first in the history of the Brazilian drama to feature [labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wage_labour) [workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laborer) as [protagonists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protagonist).[^cite_note-271]

After the military coup of 1964, plays started focusing on [Brazilian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Brazil) (Zumbi, Tiradentes). Teatro de Arena and Teatro Oficina supported the democratic resistance during the [military dictatorship](146728.md) period, marked by its censorship. The Tropicalist movement began there. A number of plays represented [historic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History) moments, notably "O Rei da Vela", "Galileu Galilei" (1968), "Na Selva das Cidades" (1969) and "Gracias Señor" (1972).

The district of Bixiga concentrates the greatest number of [theaters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater), around 40 including the theaters that are closed for refurbishing or for other reasons, and small alternatives companies venues. Some of the most important are Renault, Brigadeiro, Zaccaro, [Bibi Ferreira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibi_Ferreira), Maria della Costa, Ruth Escobar, Opera, TBC, Imprensa, Oficina, Àgora, Cacilda Becker, Sérgio Cardoso, do Bixiga, and Bandeirantes.

### Museums



[São Paulo Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Art)



[Pinacotheca of the State of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinacoteca_do_Estado_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)



[Ipiranga Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museu_Paulista)



[Catavento Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catavento_Museum)



[Latin America Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_America_Memorial)



[São Paulo Museum of Image and Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Image_and_Sound)



[Football Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Museum) at [Pacaembu Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacaembu_Stadium)



[Itaú Cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ita%C3%BA_Cultural)

São Paulo has many neighborhoods and buildings of historical value. The city has a large number of museums and art galleries. Among the museums in the city are [São Paulo Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Art) (MASP), the [Ipiranga Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museu_Paulista), the Museum of Sacred Art, the [Museum of the Portuguese Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_the_Portuguese_Language), the [Pinacoteca do Estado de São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinacoteca_do_Estado_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), among other renowned institutions. It also houses one of the top five zoos in the world, the [São Paulo Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Zoo).[^cite_note-272]

The [Ipiranga Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipiranga_Museum) was the first monument built to preserve the memory of the [Independence of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Declaration_of_Independence). It opened on September 7, 1895 as the Natural Science Museum. In 1919, it became a [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History) museum. Reflecting the architectural influence of the Versailles Palace in France, the Ipiranga's collection, with approximately 100,000 pieces, comprises works of art, furniture, clothing and appliances that belonged to those who took part in [Brazilian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Brazil), such as explorers, rulers and freedom fighters. Its facilities house a library with 100,000 books and the "Centro de Documentação Histórica", Historic Documentation Center, with 40,000 [manuscripts](19167.md).

The [Ema Gordon Klabin Cultural Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ema_Gordon_Klabin_Cultural_Foundation) opened to the public in March 2007. Its headquarters is a 1920s mansion. It houses 1545 works, including paintings by [Marc Chagall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Chagall), [Pompeo Batoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pompeo_Batoni), [Pierre Gobert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Gobert) and [Frans Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frans_Post), Brazilian modernists [Tarsila do Amaral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarsila_do_Amaral), [Di Cavalcanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Di_Cavalcanti) and [Portinari](430593.md), period furniture, decorative and archeological pieces.

Stretching over 78 thousand square meters (0.84 million square feet), [Memorial da América Latina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_da_Am%C3%A9rica_Latina) (*Latin America's Memorial*) was conceived to showcase [Latin American countries](18524.md) and their roots and cultures. It is home to the headquarters of Parlamento Latino-Americano – Parlatino (Latin American Parliament). Designed by [Oscar Niemeyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Niemeyer), Memorial has an exhibition pavilion with permanent exhibition of the continent's craftwork production; a library with books, newspapers, magazines, videos, films and records about the history of Latin America; and a 1,679-seat auditorium.

Hospedaria do Imigrante (*Immigrant's Hostel*) was built in 1886 and opened in 1887. Immigrant's Hostel was built in Brás to welcome the immigrants who arrived in Brazil through the [Port of Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Santos), quarantining those who were sick and helping new arrivals to find work in coffee plantations in Western, Northern and Southwestern São Paulo State and Northern Paraná State. From 1882 to 1978, 2.5 million immigrants of more than 60 nationalities and ethnicities were guests there,[^cite_note-Hospedaria_dos_Imigrantes_1885-273] all of them duly registered in the museum's books and lists. The hostel hosted approximately 3,000 people on average, but occasionally reached 8,000. The hostel received the last immigrants in 1978.[^cite_note-274] In 1998 the hostel became a museum, where it preserves the immigrants' documentation, memory and objects. Located in one of the few remaining centenarian buildings, the museum occupies part of the former hostel. The museum also restores wooden train wagons from the former [São Paulo Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Railway). Two restored wagons inhabit the museum. One dates from 1914, while a second class passenger car dates from 1931. The museum records the names of all immigrants who were hosted there from 1888 to 1978.[^cite_note-275]

[MASP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Museum_of_Art) has one of world's most important collections of [European art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_art_history). The most important collections cover Italian and French painting schools. The museum was founded by [Assis Chateaubriand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assis_Chateaubriand) and is directed by [Pietro Maria Bardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Maria_Bardi). Its headquarters, opened in 1968, were designed by [Lina Bo Bardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lina_Bo_Bardi). MASP organizes temporary exhibitions in special areas. Brazilian and international exhibitions of [contemporary arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_art), [photography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photography), [design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Design) and [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture) take turn during the whole year.[^cite_note-276]

Located next to the Luz metro station, the [Pinacoteca do Estado de São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinacoteca_do_Estado_de_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) was projected by architect [Ramos de Azevedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramos_de_Azevedo) in 1895. It was constructed to house an Arts Lyceum. In 1911, it became a museum, where it hosts a number of art exhibitions, such as bronze statues of French sculptor [Auguste Rodin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auguste_Rodin) took place in 2001. There is also a permanent exhibition on the "Resistance" movement that took place during military dictatorship in the Republican period, including a reconstructed prison cell where political prisoners were kept.

The [Catavento Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catavento_Museum) is an interactive museum, inaugurated in 2009. It is dedicated to science and its dissemination, and is located in the [Palácio das Indústrias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_das_Ind%C3%BAstrias) ("Palace of the Industries"). The 12,000 square meter space is divided into 4 sections: "Universo" ("Universe"),"Vida" ("Life"), "Engenho" ("Ingenuity") and "Sociedade" ("Society")[^cite_note-277] and has more than 250 installations. Aimed at young audiences, it was founded by the state secretariats of culture and education, with an investment of 20 million reais after 14 months of construction.[^cite_note-278]

The [Museum of Art of the Parliament of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Art_of_the_Parliament_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) is a contemporary art museum housed in the Palácio 9 de Julho, the Legislative Assembly of São Paulo house. The museum is run by the Department of Artistic Heritage of the Legislative Assembly and has paintings, sculpture, prints, ceramics and photographs, exploring the Brazilian contemporary art.

### Media



Headquarters of the newspaper *[O Estado de S. Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Estado_de_S._Paulo)* on the [Marginal Tietê](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marginal_Tiet%C3%AA)

São Paulo is home to the two most important daily newspapers in Brazil, *[Folha de S.Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folha_de_S.Paulo)* and *[O Estado de S. Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Estado_de_S._Paulo)*. Also, the top three weekly news magazines of the country are based in the city, *[Veja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veja_\(magazine\))*, *[Época](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89poca_\(Brazilian_magazine\))* and *[ISTOÉ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISTO%C3%89)*.

Two of the five major television networks are based in the city, [Band](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rede_Bandeirantes) and [RecordTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RecordTV), while [SBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistema_Brasileiro_de_Televis%C3%A3o) and [RedeTV!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RedeTV!) are based in [Osasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osasco), a city in the [São Paulo metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), while [Globo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rede_Globo), the country's most watched TV channel, has a major news bureau and entertainment production center in the city. In addition, [Gazeta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV_Gazeta) is at [Paulista Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulista_Avenue) and the city is used for its station idents since 2014.

Many of the major AM and FM radio networks of Brazil are headquartered in São Paulo, such as [Jovem Pan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jovem_Pan), Rádio Mix, [Transamérica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transam%C3%A9rica_Pop), [BandNews FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BandNews_FM), [CBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Brasileira_de_Not%C3%ADcias), 89 A Radio Rock, Kiss FM and [Band FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grupo_Bandeirantes_de_Comunica%C3%A7%C3%A3o). The telephone area code for the city of São Paulo is 11.[^cite_note-279]

### Sports

See also: [Football in São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_S%C3%A3o_Paulo), [Sport in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Brazil), and [Campeonato Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campeonato_Paulista)



[Formula One](10854.md) [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) is held at the [Autódromo José Carlos Pace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aut%C3%B3dromo_Jos%C3%A9_Carlos_Pace) in [Interlagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interlagos).



The [Esporte Clube Pinheiros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esporte_Clube_Pinheiros) is an important multi-sports and social club.



[Saint Silvester Road Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Silvester_Road_Race) in 2018

The city hosts sporting events of national and international importance, such as the [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix), held at the [Interlagos Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interlagos_Circuit). Among the main events that São Paulo hosted are the [1950 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_FIFA_World_Cup), the [1963 Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_Pan_American_Games), the [2000 FIFA Club World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_FIFA_Club_World_Championship), the [2014 FIFA World Cup](656933.md) [Opening Ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_opening_ceremony) (and five more matches from the same tournament) and The city also has a [Jockey Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jockey_Club), where the first race took place on 29 October 1876.[^cite_note-280]

As in the rest of Brazil, [football](10568.md) is the most popular sport. The city's major teams are [Corinthians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_Club_Corinthians_Paulista), [Palmeiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sociedade_Esportiva_Palmeiras) and [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_FC). [Portuguesa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associa%C3%A7%C3%A3o_Portuguesa_de_Desportos) is a medium club and [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_Atl%C3%A9tico_Juventus), [Nacional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nacional_Atl%C3%A9tico_Clube_\(SP\)) and [Barcelona EC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barcelona_Esportivo_Capela) are three small clubs.[^cite_note-281]

[Formula One](10854.md) is also one of the most popular sports in Brazil. Three-time Formula One world champion and São Paulo native [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna) is one of Brazil's most famous sportsmen. The Formula One [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) (formally known as the [Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Grand_Prix)) is held at the [Autódromo José Carlos Pace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aut%C3%B3dromo_Jos%C3%A9_Carlos_Pace) in [Interlagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interlagos), [Socorro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socorro_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)). The Grand Prix has been held at the Interlagos circuit from [1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Brazilian_Grand_Prix) to 1977, in 1979 and 1980, and from [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Brazilian_Grand_Prix) to the present. Four Brazilian drivers have won the Brazilian Grand Prix, all of whom were born in São Paulo: [Emerson Fittipaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerson_Fittipaldi) ([1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Brazilian_Grand_Prix) and [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Brazilian_Grand_Prix)), [José Carlos Pace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Pace) ([1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975_Brazilian_Grand_Prix)), Ayrton Senna ([1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Brazilian_Grand_Prix) and [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Brazilian_Grand_Prix)) and [Felipe Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Massa) ([2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Brazilian_Grand_Prix) and [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Brazilian_Grand_Prix)). In [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), a new local railway station *Autódromo* of the Line C (Line 9) of [CPTM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Companhia_Paulista_de_Trens_Metropolitanos), was constructed near the circuit to improve access.

Volleyball, basketball, skateboard and tennis are other major sports. There are several traditional [sports clubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_club) in São Paulo that are home for teams in many championships. The most important are [Esporte Clube Pinheiros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esporte_Clube_Pinheiros) (waterpolo, [women's volleyball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esporte_Clube_Pinheiros_\(women's_volleyball\)), swimming, [men's basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esporte_Clube_Pinheiros_\(basketball\)) and [handball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_handball)), [Clube Athletico Paulistano (basketball)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_Athletico_Paulistano_\(basketball\)), Esporte Clube Banespa (volleyball, handball and [futsal](188197.md)), [Esporte Clube Sírio (basketball)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esporte_Clube_S%C3%ADrio_\(basketball\)), Associação Atlética Hebraica (basketball), Clube Atlético Monte Líbano (basketball), Clube de Campo Associação Atlética Guapira (amateur football) and Clube Atlético Ipiranga (multi-sports and former professional football).

The [São Silvestre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Silvester_Road_Race) Race takes place every New Year's Eve. It was first held in 1925, when the competitors ran about 8,000 meters (26,000 feet). Since then, the distance raced varied, but is now set at 15 km (9.3 mi). The [São Paulo Indy 300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Indy_300) was an [IndyCar Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyCar_Series) race in [Santana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santana_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)) that ran annually from 2010 to 2013. The event was removed from the [2014 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_IndyCar_Series_season) calendar. São Paulo hosted the official [1984 Tournament of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Tournament_of_the_Americas) (basketball) where the [Brazilian national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil_men's_national_basketball_team) won its first out of four gold medals.

In Bom Retiro district, there is a public baseball stadium, [Estádio Mie Nishi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A1dio_Municipal_de_Beisebol_Mie_Nishi), while Santo Amaro district is the seat of the Núcleo de Alto Rendimento (NAR) is a high performance sports center focused on Olympic athletes. São Paulo is also [rugby union](25405.md)'s stronghold in Brazil, with the main rugby field in the city being at the [São Paulo Athletic Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Athletic_Club), São Paulo's oldest club, founded by the British community. The [Cobras Brasil Rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobras_Brasil_XV), Brazilian professional franchise that plays the [Super Rugby Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Rugby_Americas), is based in São Paulo.

The city has five major stadiums: [Morumbi Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morumbi_Stadium), owned by [São Paulo FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_FC);[^cite_note-282] [Pacaembu Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacaembu_Stadium), owned by the municipal administration;[^cite_note-283] the [Allianz Parque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz_Parque) arena by [S.E. Palmeiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.E._Palmeiras);[^cite_note-Arena_-_Allianz_Parque-284] [Canindé Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canind%C3%A9_Stadium), owned by [Portuguesa de Desportos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguesa_de_Desportos)[^cite_note-cbfstadium-285] and [Arena Corinthians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arena_Corinthians), owned by [Sport Club Corinthians Paulista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_Club_Corinthians_Paulista), located in [Itaquera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itaquera_\(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo\)).[^cite_note-FifaHost-286] It also has several volleyball and basketball gyms, tennis courts, and many other sports arenas, such as the [Ginásio do Ibirapuera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gin%C3%A1sio_do_Ibirapuera), intended mainly for athletics.[^cite_note-Ginásio-287]
